Kindle Help Guide

Amazon’s official help for finding and reading Kindle books, gathered into one screen-reader-friendly document

2026-07-31

Kindle Help Guide

This guide gathers the official help Amazon publishes about finding, obtaining and reading Kindle books, taken from Amazon’s own customer service pages on 31 July 2026. The words are Amazon’s; nothing has been summarised or rewritten.

Kindle books can be read on a Kindle e-reader, in the Kindle app for iPhone and iPad, in the app for Windows, on the web at read.amazon.com, and — for titles with Audible narration — aloud through Alexa. All of those are in scope. Amazon’s shopping, ordering, delivery and Prime Video help is not.

This is the second edition, and it is a very different document from the first. Amazon files its help under numbered nodes whose addresses say nothing about their subject, so a gathering run cannot tell a page about Kindle books from a page about parcel delivery until it has fetched it. The first run collected 800 pages, of which 134 were on the subject, and stopped at its own limit with more than two thousand addresses still waiting — nearly all of them the rest of Amazon’s customer service estate rather than anything about books.

The second run followed links only from pages that were themselves about reading. It gathered 2,339 pages, of which 261 are about Kindle books and reading and are here. That is two and a half times the first edition.

Some of what remains is still about Amazon’s wider device estate, because the line is genuinely blurred: a page about the screen reader on a Fire tablet is about reading, and a page about registering a device is about registering the device you read on. Where a page mentions Kindle it is kept, even if it also mentions something else. 13 pages were left out on top of that, and they are worth naming: Amazon publishes the open-source licences for its reading software as help articles, and they are lists of software package names and licence text rather than guidance. One runs to 297,000 characters. Together they were two fifths of this document. The safety and compliance pages, which are real device documentation, are kept.

The sections are editorial, not Amazon’s. A help node’s address is a number, and Amazon publishes no grouping that survives the journey, so the articles are grouped by subject, alphabetically, with Miscellaneous last.

Illustrations do not survive as pictures; where one carried a description it is kept as a short italic note. Amazon’s navigation, its “Was this information helpful” strip and its contact panels have been removed.

Headings run six deep at most. Level one is this title, level two is a section, level three is an article, and anything below that came from inside the article itself.

Accessible Reading Options for Kindle Reading Apps

Books that support visual adjustments allow you to modify the appearance of the text and the page layout. You can adjust the visual appearance, which can increase readability and make it easier to comprehend the text.

Visual Adjustment Options for Kindle for Android and iOS Apps

You can access the visual adjustment options from the reading settings Aa menu in your Kindle apps for Android, and iOS/macOS.

To access the reading settings Aa menu:

  1. Open your Kindle app for Android or iOS.
  2. Open a Kindle book.
  3. Select the top center of your screen.
  4. Select the reading settings Aa menu and then choose the tab with the feature you want to adjust.

From the Font tab, you can edit:

  • Font Size: Options to increase or decrease the default font size of the text.
  • Font Type: Choose from various font styles, including Amazon Ember Bold and OpenDyslexic.
  • Spacing: Increase or decrease the space between lines of text, paragraphs, words, and characters.
  • Screen Brightness: Use the slider to adjust the screen background light from dimmer to brighter. Tip: You can adjust screen brightness from all four tabs.

From the Layout tab, you can edit:

  • Page Color: Choose between white, sepia, light green, and black, for different levels of contrast.
  • Dark Mode: If you use Dark mode on your device, Kindle apps for Android and iOS display Dark mode as well.
  • Continuous Scrolling: Read by scrolling vertically instead of swiping pages.
  • Margin Adjustment: Make the book margins wider or narrower.
  • Text Alignment: Choose between left-aligned or justified text.
  • Orientation lock: On some devices, you can prevent your screen from changing between Portrait and Landscape modes.

From the Themes tab, you can create a custom theme or select a preset one:

  • Custom Theme: Create your own favorite settings and save the theme. You can have multiple custom themes. Start by saving your theme with a name, and then when you change any setting, you receive the option to save the new theme.
  • Preset Theme options: Compact, Standard, Large, and Low Vision.

From the More tab, you can choose from various features to assist you with reading:

  • Assistive Reader: Listen to Text-to-Speech and read along with highlighted text. To learn more about using Assistive Reader, go to How to Use the Assistive Reader in Kindle Apps.
  • Real-time Text Highlighting: Highlights words while either Assistive Reader or Audible narrates your text.
  • Reading Ruler: To help guide your reading, add a colored ruler over the text.
  • Word Wise (on Android): Get simple definitions and synonyms displayed inline above more difficult words while you read.

Visual Adjustment Options for Kindle for Web

  • Font Type: Choose from three font styles: Bookerly, Amazon Ember, and OpenDyslexic.
  • Font Size: Options to increase or decrease the default font size of the text.
  • Page Color: Choose between white, sepia, light green, and black, for different levels of contrast.
  • Layout: Choose between two columns or a single column format.
  • Margin Adjustment: Make the book margins wider or narrower.

Visual Adjustment Options for PC

  • Font Type: Choose from various font styles, including Amazon Ember Bold and OpenDyslexic.
  • Font Size: Options to increase or decrease the default font size of the text.
  • Alignment: Choose between left-aligned or justified text.
  • Spacing: Increase or decrease the space between lines of text, paragraphs, words, and characters.
  • Page Color (Color Mode): Choose between white, sepia, and black, for different levels of contrast.

Visual Adjustment Options for macOS

From the Font tab, you can edit:

  • Font Size: Options to increase or decrease the default font size of the text.
  • Font Type: Choose from various font styles, including Amazon Ember Bold and OpenDyslexic.
  • Spacing: Increase or decrease the space between lines, paragraphs, words, and characters.
  • Screen Brightness: Use the slider to adjust the screen background light from dimmer to brighter. Tip: You can adjust screen brightness from all four tabs.

From the Layout tab, you can edit:

  • Page Color: Choose between white, sepia, light green, and black, for different levels of contrast.
  • Continuous Scrolling: Read by scrolling vertically instead of swiping pages.
  • Text Alignment: Choose between left-aligned or justified text.
  • Margin Adjustment: Make the book margins wider or narrower.
  • Column: Choose between two columns or a single column format.

From the Themes tab, you can create a custom theme or select a preset one:

  • Custom Theme: Create your own favorite settings and save the theme. You can have multiple custom themes.
  • Preset Theme options: Compact, Standard, Large, and Low Vision.

From the More tab, you can choose from various features to assist you with reading:

  • Assistive Reader: Listen to Text-to-Speech and read along with highlighted text. To learn more about using Assistive Reader, go to How to Use the Assistive Reader in Kindle Apps.
  • Real-time Text Highlighting: Highlights words while either Assistive Reader or Audible narrates your text.
  • Reading Ruler: To help guide your reading, add a colored ruler over the text.

Tip: In addition to visual adjustments to your content, you can add audio with Immersion Reading. Using Audible’s Whispersync with Voice, Immersion Reading allows you to simultaneously read a Kindle book while listening to the Audible version of the book. For more information on Immersion Reading, visit Listen with Whispersync for Voice.

Buy Audible audiobooks for others

You can purchase Audible audiobooks in bulk with an Amazon Business account, distribute them using redemption codes, and track redemption status.

To buy Audible audiobooks for others:

  1. Sign in to your Amazon Business account.
  2. Go to the Audible audiobook’s product detail page.
  3. Select the quantity that you want in the Buy for others box.
  4. After you place your order, go to your order details and select Manage audiobook.
  5. Copy and share redemption instructions with recipients. Note: Only recipients in the US can use redemption codes. You cannot resell redemption codes.
**For Amazon Business customers:** If you have an Amazon Business account and need assistance with your bulk audiobook order, .

**For individual Audible users:** For help with your Audible account, your purchases and listening to audiobooks, or to contact Audible Customer Care, visit the Audible Help Center .

Download the VoiceView Audio File for Your Kindle

If you’ve recently factory reset your Kindle, you need to re-download the audio file for your device to use VoiceView.

To determine which Kindle e-reader model you’re using, refer to our page Identify Your Kindle E-Reader.

Note: VoiceView over Bluetooth is available in the following languages: English, French, German, Italian, Portuguese, and Spanish.

To download and install the audio file:

  1. Check your software version. If your Kindle is not on the latest software version, you need to update your device to the latest available version. To learn more, go to Kindle E-Reader Software Updates.
  2. Download the audio file for your device model using the following links: - Audio file for Kindle Scribe Colorsoft (1st Generation) - Audio file for Kindle Scribe (3rd Generation) - Audio file for Kindle Scribe - 2024 release - Audio file for Kindle Colorsoft (1st Generation), Kindle Colorsoft Signature (1st Generation), and Kindle Colorsoft Kids (1st Generation) - Audio file for Kindle Paperwhite (12th Generation), Kindle Paperwhite Signature (12th Generation), and Kindle Paperwhite (12th Generation) Kids Edition - 2024 release - Audio file for Kindle (11th Generation) and Kindle (11th Generation) Kids Edition - 2024 release - Audio file for Kindle Scribe - 2022 release - Audio file for Kindle (11th Generation) and Kindle (11th Generation) Kids Edition - 2022 release - Audio file for Kindle Paperwhite (11th Generation), Kindle Paperwhite (11th Generation) Signature Edition, and Kindle Paperwhite (11th Generation) Kids Edition - Audio file for Kindle Oasis (10th Generation) - Audio file for Kindle (10th Generation) and Kindle (10th Generation) Kids Edition - Audio file for Kindle Paperwhite (10th Generation) - Audio file for Kindle Oasis (9th Generation) - Audio file for Kindle (8th Generation) - Audio file for Kindle Oasis (8th Generation) - Audio file for Kindle Paperwhite (7th Generation) - Audio file for Kindle Voyage (7th Generation)
  3. Turn on your Kindle and plug it into your computer using the USB cable.
  4. Drag and drop the file from your computer into the Kindle drive.
  5. Monitor the transfer to ensure the file transfers successfully.
  6. Eject the Kindle to safely disconnect your Kindle from your computer, and then disconnect the USB cable from your Kindle and your computer.
  7. Hold the power button on your Kindle for 20 seconds.
  8. Wait one minute while your Kindle restarts and installs the voice file.

Enlarge the Font Size on Your Kindle E-Reader

Make the font on your Kindle device bigger, or get easier-to-read Kindle content by changing the size of your text.

Note: Only Kindle E-Reader 11th Generation and higher, Kindle Scribe, and Kindle Colorsoft support device font size changes.

To change the Font Size on your Kindle:

  1. Go to Settings.
  2. Select Screen and brightness, then select Display & text size.
  3. Select the desired font size. On Kindle Scribe, drag or tap on the slider to select the font size you want.

To change the Font Size on your eBook:

  1. Open a book on your Kindle.
  2. Tap the top of the screen to bring up the reading toolbar.
  3. Select the Aa icon.
  4. Choose the text size appropriate for your reading comfort level.

You can also adjust font size by pinching in or out on the page while reading a book.

Guide to Reading Books with VoiceView on Fire Tablet

Use gestures and Text-to-Speech to read and interact with your book on Fire tablet (FireOS 5 and higher).

You can switch between two modes of reading: continuous and non-continuous. Continuous reading will read all pages in the book, while non-continuous reading will read a page, character, or word on the page.

  • Adjust text selection
    • Directions: Selection markers are available in the menu after selecting text. Select the markers to increase or decrease the text selection size.
  • Change continuous reading speed
    • Directions: The continuous reading speed control is located in the bottom right corner of the screen when the reading toolbar is open. Tap the control, and then double-tap the screen to change the reading speed.
  • Change reading voice
    • Directions: With the reading toolbar open, swipe down from the top of the screen with two fingers to open Quick Actions , and then select Settings . From Settings , select Keyboard & Language , and then select Text-to-Speech .
  • Enable heading navigation
    • Directions: Perform a swipe up and down gesture until “Heading Navigation” is chose as your level of granularity.
  • Open the Go To menu
    • Directions: Swipe from the left edge of the screen with two fingers, and then double-tap a chapter or section.
  • Read additional content
    • Directions: Double tap to select highlights, footnotes, alternate text, and Word Wise content when they are announced. Swipe left or right to read text before or after selected content.
  • Read one page, paragraph, character, heading, or word at a time
    • Directions: Swipe up and then right in a continuous motion with a finger to activate the Local Context Menu . With the Local Context Menu open, select your preferred reading level: character, word, line, paragraph, page, heading, or default. Depending on the content being read, some reading level options may not be available. Note: The VoiceView’s Text-to-Speech voice reads in your device language. However, not all languages are supported.
  • Select text
    • Directions: Explore the screen with a finger to find the word you want to select. Double-tap the word, and then hold to listen to the definition of the word being read to you. You can also swipe to the left or right to choose a highlight color, add a note, share your notes and highlights, and more.
  • Show reading toolbar
    • Directions: While reading, tap the screen, and then double-tap the screen to show the reading toolbar.
  • Start reading
    • Directions: From your book, swipe down from the top of the page with two fingers when the reading toolbar is closed to start reading from the top of the page. Use an L gesture to start reading from the current focus point. When you open a book, Text-to-Speech begins reading the book and automatically turns the pages.
  • Switch from continuous to non-continuous reading
    • Directions: Tap the screen to switch from reading continuously to reading one page at a time. When using VoiceView, granularity can be adjusted to read one word, line, heading, or character at a time Note: Depending on the content being read, some reading level options may not be available.
  • Turn page
    • Directions: Swipe left with two fingers to go to the next page. Swipe right with two fingers to go to the previous page.

Guide to VoiceView Gestures on Your Kindle

Use VoiceView gestures to navigate a supported Kindle and listen to Kindle books and other Kindle content over a compatible Bluetooth audio device.

To determine which Kindle e-reader model you’re using, refer to our page Identify Your Kindle E-Reader.

Note: VoiceView over Bluetooth is available in the following languages: English, French, German, Italian, Portuguese, and Spanish.

  • Explore menus and items
    • Gesture: Drag a finger over each item on the screen.
  • Decrease granularity
    • Gesture: Swipe down then up using a single motion.
  • Go to next item by granularity
    • Gesture: Swipe down using one finger.
  • Go to previous item by granularity
    • Gesture: Swipe up using one finger.
  • Go to the next item
    • Gesture: Swipe right using a finger.
  • Go to the next page
    • Gesture: Swipe left with two fingers.
  • Go to the previous item
    • Gesture: Swipe left using a finger.
  • Go to the previous page
    • Gesture: Swipe right with two fingers.
  • Hear additional information about an item
    • Gesture: Double tap on the item and hold for one second. After you hear a tone, drag your finger over the pop-up menu to navigate.
  • Increase granularity
    • Gesture: Swipe up then down using a single motion.
  • Open an item
    • Gesture: Select the item, and then double tap anywhere on the screen. If the item doesn’t open, try slowing down or speeding up your tapping speed.
  • Pair a new Bluetooth audio device
    • Gesture: Press the power button 3 times to initiate the search for a new Bluetooth audio device. Hold two fingers on screen to select the Bluetooth audio device.
  • Pause VoiceView while reading
    • Gesture: Tap on the screen with two fingers at the same time. To resume, use the Start Reading gesture (swipe down and to the right in an “L” shape).
  • Turn on VoiceView again if turned off or in Airplane mode
    • Gesture: Press the power button 3 times to reconnect to a previously Bluetooth audio device.
  • Scroll up or down
    • Gesture: Swipe up or down with two fingers. Note: This feature only works when you can scroll on the screen.
  • Show reading toolbar
    • Gesture: While reading, double-tap the screen to show the reading toolbar.
  • Start reading
    • Gesture: When you open a book, VoiceView begins reading the book and automatically turns the pages. If the reading stops and you need to start it again, swipe down and to the right with one finger (an “L” shape). This will initiate continuous reading again.
  • Type on the keyboard
    • Gesture: Move a finger slowly over the keys. When you hear the letter you want to select, lift your finger from the keyboard to add that letter.

How to Use the Assistive Reader in Kindle Apps

Assistive Reader provides you with new options to control TTS (Text-to-Speech) on the free Kindle app for iPhone, iPad, Android phones and tablets, Mac, and Fire tablets.

Assistive Reader Basics

With Assistive Reader, you can use built-in audio controls in the Kindle app, as well as real-time text highlighting while listening.

Assistive Reader uses your device’s OS-level Text-to-Speech engines to read aloud the text of the book.

Assistive Reader automatically pauses when you’re no longer reading, such as when you open the navigation menu or make an annotation (highlights or notes).

Assistive Reader works with any Kindle book with Enhanced Typesetting. To learn if a book supports Enhanced Typesetting, go to the Kindle eBook Store and look for “Enhanced Typesetting: Enabled” in the features list.

The Assistive Reader supports books that are:

  • Purchased from the Kindle store; or
  • Borrowed from Kindle Unlimited or Prime Reading, or other official Kindle partner sources; or
  • Shared with you in your Family Library.

Books must also be available in a language supported by your device’s Text-to-Speech engine.

Turn On Assistive Reader

  1. Open the Kindle app on your iOS, Android, or Fire Tablet device.
  2. Open the content where you’d like to use Assistive Reader. Once you turn on Assistive Reader, it remains available until you turn it off again.
  3. Select the top center of the screen, and then the reading settings menu, Aa.
  4. Select More, and then turn on Assistive Reader.
  5. To display the player controls, tap the screen. You can control the reading speed, or rewind by 30 seconds using the player controls at the bottom of the screen.

Tip: To start Assistive Reader from a specific point in the text, long press a word to open the quick actions menu. Then, select Play to begin Text-to-Speech from that point forward, rather than from the top of the page.

  • Accessibility for Devices and Digital Services
  • Accessibility on Kindle E-Reader
  • Accessibility on Kindle Reading Apps

How to Use the Assistive Reader on Your Kindle

Assistive Reader provides you with new options to control TTS (Text-to-Speech) on your Kindle E-Reader 11th Generation and higher, Kindle Scribe, or Kindle Colorsoft.

Listen to Your Books with Assistive Reader

Note: Assistive Reader supports books in English, Spanish, French, German, and Italian language.

Assistive Reader lets you listen to your Kindle books while following along with text underlining. With this feature you can:

  • Play, pause, control the reading speed, or rewind by 30 seconds using the player controls at the bottom of the screen
  • Follow along with the words highlighted as they’re read aloud
  • Pick up right where you left off - Assistive Reader automatically pauses when you open the navigation menu or create highlights or notes

Assistive Reader only works with books that have Enhanced Typesetting. To check if a book has Enhanced Typesetting:

  1. Go to the Kindle Store.
  2. Find your book.
  3. Look for “Enhanced Typesetting: Enabled” in the book’s features list.

Turn On Assistive Reader

  1. Open the content where you’d like to use Assistive Reader.
  2. Tap the top center of the screen, and then select the reading settings menu, Aa.
  3. Select More, and then turn on Assistive Reader.

Note: Once you turn on Assistive Reader, it remains available until you turn it off again.

Kindle for Windows Accessibility Features

Kindle for Windows supportsthe Narrator screen reader that’s built into Windows 11, NVDA 2017.1 or higher and JAWS screen reader on Windows computers.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

The Kindle for PC accessibility features provide the following functions:

  • You can read aloud over 6 million titles in English and most titles available in other languages in the Kindle Store using screen reader technology.
  • Navigate within your library or within a book, with consistent title, menu and button names.
  • Read character-by-character, word-by-word, or continuously, as well as move forward or backward in the text.
  • Search for a book within your library or search within your book and navigate to specific locations.
  • Customize the reading experience including changing the font, text size, background color, and margins.

The Kindle Store identifies supported content by major screen readers with the text Screen Reader: Supported on the product detail pages. This indicates that the book is compatible with many popular screen readers: VoiceView on Fire Tablets and Kindle E-readers, VoiceOver on iOS, TalkBack on Android, and NVDA on Windows. Functionality may vary across titles and screen readers.

Using Tables with Narrator Support

  • Navigate and exit content, including images and links, within tables in books.

For more details, visit Kindle for Windows Keyboard Shortcuts.

Learn About Accessibility Information for Kindle Books

Kindle book listings include accessibility information that you can use to determine if a book meets your needs. All accessibility information displayed for Kindle books is based on details provided by the publisher.

You can find the accessibility icon in the book details section. If you don’t find the icon, you may need to scroll to a second screen of icons. To review the book’s accessibility details, select Learn more below the accessibility icon .

  • Visual Adjustments
  • Nonvisual Reading
  • Hazards
  • Conformance
  • Navigation
  • Other Features
  • Troubleshooting

Visual Adjustments

Books that support visual adjustments allow you to modify the appearance of the text and the page layout. You can adjust the visual appearance, which can increase readability and make it easier to comprehend the text.

Kindle books come in two different formats:

  • Reflowable Kindle books allow for the modification of the appearance by adapting to your device’s screen size and your chosen font and layout settings.
  • Fixed layout books include print replica, manga, comics, and some children’s books or textbooks. With this format, books preserve their layout and structure and don’t allow for visual adjustments.

A book will include one of the following descriptions for visual adjustments:

  • Appearance can be modified: The text of this Kindle book can be adjusted (e.g., size, font, color, margins, spacing, alignment)
  • Appearance cannot be modified: The text of this Kindle book cannot be adjusted (e.g., size, font, color, margins, spacing, alignment)
  • Not known if appearance can be modified: No information is available about the capability to adjust the text of this Kindle book (e.g., size, font, color, margins, spacing alignment)

For more on accessibility features in Kindle that allow readers to modify the appearance of the text, visit Kindle Accessibility.

Nonvisual Reading

Nonvisual reading indicates whether all content required for comprehension can be consumed in text and is accessible to users of assistive technologies. Some examples of compatible assistive technologies include screen readers and refreshable braille displays, typically used by people who are blind or have low vision.

The text of most reflowable Kindle books is accessible. However, when Kindle books include content such as images, photographs, graphs, tables, or videos, textual alternatives may be required for comprehension. Without the textual alternatives, the book won’t be considered readable in read aloud and braille.

Textual alternatives could include alt text, extended descriptions, transcripts, and captions. Currently, print replica and image-based fixed layout Kindle books aren’t readable in read aloud and braille.

Note: Kindle books don’t currently support closed captions in audio/video content.

A book will include one of the following nonvisual reading descriptions:

  • Readable in read aloud and braille: The text of this Kindle book is accessible to screen readers, and all meaningful non-text content has text alternatives (e.g., images have alt text and/or extended descriptions).
  • Partially readable in read aloud and braille: The text of this Kindle book is accessible to screen readers, but some meaningful non-text content may not have text alternatives (e.g., image alt text and/or extended descriptions).
  • Not readable in read aloud and braille: The text of this Kindle book is not accessible to screen readers. Note: Print replica and image-based Kindle books (e.g., comics, manga) do not currently support nonvisual reading.
  • Not known if readable in read aloud and braille: No information available about the nonvisual reading aspects of this Kindle book.

Hazards

Hazard labels help you determine whether there are any potential hazards present in the Kindle book that could afflict physiologically sensitive readers. Potential hazards only exist within a Kindle book that contains audio and/or video content.

Note: Audio and/or video files don’t start automatically. The reader must start the file.

A book will include one of the following hazard warning descriptions:

  • This Kindle book contains no hazards: Note: This refers to books with no audio/video content, or books with video content in which the publisher has confirmed that there are no hazards present.
  • This Kindle book contains the following hazards:
    • Video with a flashing hazard: Note: This refers to video content with flashing that occurs more than 3 times per second or doesn’t meet the general flash or red flash thresholds.
    • Video with motion simulation
    • Audio
  • This Kindle book contains audio or video which may include flashing, motion simulation, or sound.
  • No information available about potential hazards in this Kindle book.

Conformance

Conformance labels help you identify whether the digital publication claims to meet internationally recognized conformance standards for accessibility.

The only time Conformance information displays is if it’s provided by the publisher. Conformance claims include the specific set of accessibility requirements that the Kindle book purports to meet and, if applicable, will include the certifier’s name. For example: WCAG 2.0/2.1/2.2 Level A/AA/AAA.

Navigation

One or more navigation labels may display if the publisher indicates that the Kindle book includes navigational features to support accessibility. Possible navigational features include:

  • Table of contents: The book includes a table of contents for navigating to the major sections of the book.
  • Index: The book contains one or more indexes of the content. Examples of possible indexes: a topical index or an index of names. Indexes allow readers who can’t see the text or have difficulty following it to find and return to subjects more easily.
  • Real page numbers that correspond to an equivalent print book: The book contains references to the page numbering of an equivalent print book.
  • Heading markup for screen reader users: The book contains heading tags so that users of assistive technologies can understand the structure of the book without having to use the table of contents.

Other Features

You can review Other Features labels to find out if the Kindle book includes one or more additional accessibility features not listed in the preceding sections. If information about the available accessibility features is provided, it’s displayed in this section. Possible features that may be included at this time are:

  • Audio/video: This Kindle book includes audio and/or video media. Note: Kindle books don’t currently support closed captions in audio/video content.
  • Extended descriptions: Complex images in this Kindle book include long descriptions for blind and low vision readers.
  • Accessible math content: MathML markup language was used to encode the mathematical equations in this Kindle book.

Troubleshooting

In general, a book that supports visual adjustments and nonvisual reading will be accessible when read with any Kindle device or app.

However, there are some limitations to be aware of. Not all Kindle book accessibility features are fully supported across all Kindle devices and apps. For example, you can’t currently read alt text and mathML on Kindle e-readers.

For more information about current support for accessibility features on our Kindle devices and apps, visit: Kindle Accessibility.

To report an error in the accessibility information of a Kindle book:

  1. Select the Report an issue link, located in every Kindle book detail page below the book description and details.
  2. Select Report beneath where it says “Does this item contain quality or formatting issues?”.
  3. Select Poor formatting.

Tip: As an example, you might report a book that’s labeled as “Readable in read aloud and braille,” but does not include text alternatives for meaningful images within the book.

Other Reading Accessibility Settings on Fire Tablet

Turn on the Reading Ruler, switch to landscape orientation for reading, use continuous scrolling or the volume buttons to turn pages with the Kindle app.

Reading Ruler

The Reading Ruler keeps your eyes focused on the text by highlighting or blocking out certain lines. It displays a customizable bar with adjustable color, opacity, style, and size.

To turn on the Reading Ruler on a Fire tablet:

  1. Open the Kindle app and select a book.

  2. Tap at the top to bring up the toolbar and select the Aa icon while reading.

  3. Select the More tab.

  4. Select Reading Ruler and switch the toggle for Reading Ruler on.

    If you’re not already in continuous scroll reading mode, you see a prompt to switch to it. Only continuous scroll reading mode supports Reading Ruler currently.

Landscape Orientation Support for Reading

Your can read Kindle books on your Fire tablet in either portrait or landscape orientation. These options allow greater flexibility in how to position your device while reading.

To activate landscape mode in the Kindle app on a Fire Tablet:

  1. Deactivate the orientation lock from the slide-down menu at the top of the screen.
  2. Rotate your Tablet to the landscape position.

Continuous Scrolling

Continuous scrolling and pagination are two optional reading modes, each of which can be beneficial based on your personal needs and preferences.

With continuous scrolling, you read your Kindle book by scrolling vertically like a webpage up/down, rather than flipping pages by swiping left/right or tapping.

To activate continuous scrolling in the Kindle app on a Fire tablet:

  1. Open the Kindle app and select a book.
  2. Open the Kindle app and select a book.Tap at the top to bring up the toolbar and select the Aa icon while reading.
  3. Select the Layout tab.
  4. Switch the toggle for Continuous Scrolling on.

Turn Pages with Volume Controls

You can use your Fire Tablet volume buttons to turn pages in the Kindle app. Using a single tap for turning pages may be more comfortable for some readers.

Note: Deactivate the Continuous Scrolling setting to activate Turn Pages with Volume Controls.

To activate this feature:

  1. Open the Kindle app and select a book.
  2. Tap at the top to bring up the toolbar and select the Aa icon while reading.
  3. Select the More tab.
  4. Switch the toggle for Turn Pages with Volume Controls on.

Pressing the volume up button advances to the next page, and pressing the volume down button goes to the previous page.

Immersion Reading with Audible on Fire Tablet

Note: This feature is available on Fire Tablets with FireOS 7 and above.

Switch between reading and listening with Audible audiobooks. Synchronized highlighting of the text is available while the audio is playing.

Immersion Reading with Audible is available on all titles in your Library where you have both the eBook and the Audible audiobook. These titles are Whispersync-for-Voice ready.

You can tell if a book has Whispersync on Audible and Amazon, the product details for an audiobook will say Whispersync for Voice-ready if it’s available. You can also look for the option to add the Audible narration on the Kindle eBook page on Amazon.

To use Immersion Reading with Audible on Fire Tablet:

  1. Open the Kindle app on your Tablet and select the book that you’d like to read and listen to.
  2. Tap on the page to invoke the option to download the Audible audiobook.
  3. Tap on the download icon at the bottom of the page.
  4. To begin listening, tap the play icon at the bottom of the page.

Customize features like narration speed from the mini player and sleep timer from the full Audible player.

You can pause the narration and switch between reading and listening without ever losing your place.

To turn off synchronized highlighting:

  1. Tap the top center of the screen, and then select the reading settings menu Aa.
  2. Select More, and then turn off Real-time Text Highlighting.

Redeem a gifted Audible audiobook

Did you receive an audiobook as a gift? You can redeem your audiobook by selecting the redemption link in your email notification. This will add the title to your library, so you can start listening on the Audible app for free.

To redeem your audiobook and start listening:

  1. Select the link in your email or visit the Audible redemption page.
  2. Enter the redemption code from your email notification.
  3. Open or download the free Audible app to start listening. Note: Only recipients in the US can use redemption codes. You cannot resell redemption codes.
**For individual Audible users:** For help with your Audible account, your purchases, redeeming gifts, and listening to audiobooks, or to contact Audible Customer Care, visit the Audible Help Center.

Send Audible audiobooks to individuals

You can purchase Audible audiobooks in bulk with an Amazon Business account, distribute them using redemption codes, and track redemption status.

To manage your bulk order and share redemption codes, go to your order details.

To share redemption codes:

  1. Sign in to your Amazon Business account and go to Your Orders.
  2. Go to order details and select Manage audiobook.
  3. Select Copy instructions to copy the redemption code and pre-written instructions you can email.
  4. Paste the redemption instructions into an email or message for your recipient.
  5. Recipients can listen using Audible’s free app. Note: Only recipients in the US can use redemption codes. You cannot resell redemption codes.

Send Audible audiobooks to multiple recipients

You can purchase Audible audiobooks in bulk with an Amazon Business account, distribute them using redemption codes, and track redemption status.

To manage your bulk order and share redemption codes, go to your order details in your Amazon Business account.

Share redemption codes with multiple recipients

Mail merge combines your redemption code spreadsheet with an email template to send codes to multiple recipients at once. To get started, download our mail merge template.

To share redemption codes with mail merge on Microsoft Outlook:

  1. Sign in to your Amazon Business account.
  2. Go to your order details, select Manage audiobooks, and download your redemption code spreadsheet.
  3. Open the spreadsheet and add recipients’ email addresses to the Recipient email column.
  4. Download the mail merge template and open it in Word.
  5. In Word, go to the Mailings tab, select Start Mail Merge, and choose Email Messages.
  6. Go to Select Recipients, select Use an Existing List, and choose your redemption code spreadsheet as the data source.
  7. In your mail merge template, select the Redemption Code Placeholder, go to Insert Merge Field, and choose Redemption Code.
  8. Select Preview Results to confirm your emails look correct.
  9. Go to Finish & Merge and select Send Email Messages.
  10. In the dialog, choose Email for the To field, choose HTML as the mail format, and click Mail Merge to Outlook to send.

To share redemption codes with mail merge on Gmail:

  1. Sign in to your Amazon Business account.
  2. Go to your order details, select Manage audiobooks, and download your redemption code spreadsheet.
  3. Open the spreadsheet in Google Sheets and add recipients’ email addresses to the Recipient email column.
  4. Go to Google Workspace Marketplace, search for and install a mail merge add-on.
  5. Open the add-on, create a new email draft, and paste the mail merge template.
  6. Connect your Google Sheet as the data source following the add-on’s instructions.
  7. In your mail merge template, select the Redemption Code Placeholder, go to the add-on’s merge field tools, and choose Redemption Code.
  8. Preview your emails to confirm they look correct.
  9. Send your email to share redemption codes with recipients.

Note: Only recipients in the US can use redemption codes. You cannot resell redemption codes.

For Amazon Business customers: If you have an Amazon Business account and need assistance with your bulk audiobook order, .

For individual Audible users: For help with your Audible account, your purchases and listening to audiobooks, or to contact Audible Customer Care, visit the Audible Help Center .

Syncing Issues with Books Read by Alexa with Kindle Assistive Reader

Kindle book doesn’t sync to the furthest page when Alexa begins reading.

Note: Kindle Assistive Reader isn’t available on Alexa+.

Alexa starts reading from the most recent page read on any device. To help set context, Alexa may start reading from the previous paragraph from your most recent page.

Try these troubleshooting steps if the Kindle Assistive Reader isn’t starting from the place you last read across any device:

  1. Open the book on the Amazon device or Kindle reading app where you were last reading.
  2. Confirm that the device has internet connectivity, and then open the book you were reading manually to the furthest page read.
  3. Select the Menu icon and sync the book manually to the furthest page read.
  4. Once the sync is complete, ask Alexa to play your Kindle book. Reading picks up from that location.
  • Update Your Sync Settings for Kindle

Troubleshoot Whispersync for Voice

Whispersync for Voice automatically syncs your audiobooks with eBooks if you own or borrow both versions. With Whispersync you can seamlessly switch between reading and listening to your book.

For syncing issues with Whispersync for Voice, make sure:

  1. Your device is connected to a wireless network.
  2. The Sync Settings option from your Amazon account is turned on.
    1. Go to Manage Your Content and Devices and select the Preferences tab.
    2. Scroll down to Sync Settings and open the drop down. Check that the setting is ON.
  3. You turn on the Sync setting on your app or device. For detailed instructions, go to: Update Your Sync Settings for Kindle.
  4. You sync your Kindle app or device.
  5. Your device software is updated to the latest software.
  6. You update your Kindle app or device to the latest version. Check your device app store for any updates.
  7. You update your Kindle book.
    1. Go to Manage Your Content and Devices and select the Content tab.
    2. Select Books and search for your title.
    3. If the title shows Update available under the details, select it and then Update.
  8. As a final step, you try removing the download for the book from your device. Sync your Kindle app or device again, and then redownload the book.

Important: You must own or borrow both the audiobook and eBook versions of a title that supports Read & Listen to use Whispersync for Voice.

  • Learn about Read & Listen

  1. Open the Alexa app .

  3. Select Settings .
  4. Select Accessibility.

Use VoiceView Over Bluetooth on Kindle

Pair your supported Bluetooth audio device with your compatible Kindle device to start VoiceView.

To determine which Kindle e-reader model you’re using,refer to our page Identify Your Kindle E-Reader.

Note: VoiceView over Bluetooth is available in the following languages: English, French, German, Italian, Portuguese, and Spanish.

Before connecting your Kindle to your accessory, please review all the setup instructions for your Bluetooth audio device. There is no audio announcement or feedback until the last step.

  • For 11th Generation and newer Kindle E-Readers:
    1. Place your Bluetooth audio device into pairing mode.
    2. Press the Power button three times within two seconds to initiate VoiceView.
    3. When you hear audio from your Bluetooth device (within two minutes), hold two fingers spaced apart on the screen to confirm the pairing.
  • For previous generation Kindle E-readers:
    1. Place your Bluetooth audio device into pairing mode.
    2. Press and hold the power button on your Kindle for nine seconds. Then press two fingers spaced apart on the screen for one second.
    3. When you hear audio from your Bluetooth device (within two minutes), hold two fingers spaced apart on the screen to confirm the pairing.
    After pairing to a Bluetooth audio device, VoiceView will save the connection.
  • There are a few ways to turn off or suspend VoiceView on your Kindle.
    • Turn off your Bluetooth audio device.
    • On your Kindle, select the Quick Actions menu at the top of the screen, and then double tap to open the menu. Select VoiceView Screen Reader, and then select and double tap Off.
    • To suspend VoiceView press the power button on your Kindle. VoiceView resumes within around five seconds when you wake the Kindle. To wake, press the Kindle power button once, and then double tap on the screen.
  • Pair a Bluetooth Audio Device with Your Kindle E-Reader
  • Download the VoiceView Audio File for Your Kindle

What is Word Wise?

Get simple definitions and synonyms displayed inline above more difficult words while you read.

Word Wise is only available in books in English and isn’t enabled on all Kindle books. You can verify whether the feature is available by checking for “Word Wise: Enabled” or “Word Wise: Not Enabled” on the product detail page of the Kindle book.

Use Word Wise on the following devices and Kindle reading apps with the latest software version:

  • Any Fire tablet (3rd Generation and newer)
  • Any Kindle e-reader (6th Generation and newer)
  • Kindle for Android

If you’d like, you can deactivate Word Wise from within your book. Tap the top of the screen to access the menu, then choose the Aa menu and select More. Scroll down to Word Wise and switch the option to off.

Note: Kindle Assistive Reader isn’t available on Alexa+.

Kindle Assistive Reader supports books that are:

  • Purchased from the Kindle Store

  • Shared with you in your Amazon Family Library

Books must also support Enhanced Typesetting and be available in one of Alexa’s supported languages.

To see a list of supported books, ask Alexa to “Show my Kindle books,” or use the Alexa app.

Buy Books on Your Kindle E-Reader

Use the Kindle or the Audible Store to purchase books from your device.

The Kindle and Audible stores offer a wide selection of Kindle books and Audible audiobooks.

Audible is available in: the U.S., Brazil, Canada, Australia, India, Japan, the UK, France, Italy, Spain, and Germany.

  • From the home screen of your Kindle, select the Store icon next to the search bar.
  • Search for a title, browse by category, or check out the latest best sellers. You can also access details about titles, read customer reviews or get recommendations personalized just for you. The Kindle Store also lets you download book samples.
  • Complete your purchase by selecting an approved payment method.

After you order, the Amazon Whispernet service delivers the item directly to your Kindle via your internet connection. Books are downloaded to your Kindle immediately.

Note: The Kindle Store is not available on devices without touch screens. You can still purchase books on the Amazon website using a phone or desktop web browser and deliver them to your Kindle.

You can combine Kindle books with physical items in your cart when shopping on Amazon’s website through any browser. If you want to use available credits for your Kindle books purchase, you need to buy them using the 1-Click purchase option.

Buy Kindle Books for Others

You can use your Amazon account to purchase Kindle books in bulk and distribute them to others.

Note:

  • Kindle eBooks can only be redeemed by recipients in your same country. Recipients outside your country cannot redeem any Kindle eBooks sent to them. You can distribute any unredeemed copies to other recipients or request a refund for unredeemed copies. Only unredeemed copies can be refunded. For more information about refunds, check out Return Unredeemed Kindle Book Gift Orders.

To buy Kindle books for others:

  1. Go to the Kindle eBook’s product detail page on Amazon.
  2. In the Buy for others box select the quantity you want to purchase.
  3. Select the Buy for others button and then enter the details for your gift recipients. You can specify recipient email addresses on the checkout page to send each recipient an email with the link to redeem the eBook. Any eBooks not sent are available, after completing your purchase, to be sent when you choose.
If you didn't provide a recipient email address, instructions on how to manage your books are emailed to you after the order is complete. Go to [Send Kindle Books to Individual Recipients](#send-kindle-books-to-individual-recipients) for more information.

To print a receipt for your gift order:

  1. Go to Tus pedidos digitales, and find the gift order.
  2. Select Show Receipt, and then Print this page for your records.

Note: Kindle books and redemption links cannot be resold.

Cancel a Comixology Unlimited Subscription

Follow these steps to cancel your Comixology Unlimited membership.

  1. Go to Your Memberships & Subscriptions and sign into your Amazon account.
  2. Under Comixology Unlimited Membership, select Cancel Subscription. Your membership remains active until the next billing date. After the billing date passes, you lose access to content that you have borrowed.

Cancel Your Kindle Unlimited Subscription

Follow these steps to cancel your Kindle Unlimited membership.

Want to cancel your Kindle Unlimited membership? Do it from here:

Kindle Unlimited Central

Important: You’ll not receive a partial refund for unused months for a prepaid (gift or bundle) Kindle Unlimited membership.

  1. Go to Manage your Kindle Unlimited Membership and sign into your Amazon account.
  2. Under Your Membership, select Cancel membership. Your membership remains active until the next billing date. After the billing date passes, you lose access to content that you have checked out.

Tip: You can also choose to pause your monthly subscription for a period of 1 month, instead of cancelling. The pause becomes effective at the end of the billing cycle. Your subscription resumes automatically, or you can resume it anytime while paused. You can’t pause your subscription while on a free trial, or promotional period.

If you pause your subscription, you can resume it from .

Cancel Your Subscribe & Save Subscription

You can cancel your subscription through Your Subscribe & Save in Your Account.

To cancel your Subscribe & Save subscription:

  1. Go to Your Subscribe & Save Items.
  2. Select the Subscriptions tab.
  3. Select the product image or select Edit to view Subscription details.
  4. Select Cancel subscription.
  5. Optional: Select a cancellation reason to help us to improve your experience.
  6. Select Cancel my subscription.

Note: For your subscription cancellation to take effect from your upcoming delivery, make the required changes before the Last day to update this order date mentioned corresponding to your subscription on the Subscribe & Save page. Once an order has entered the shipping process, it can no longer be canceled. The next shipment on your subscribed orders schedule is canceled in this case. Note that if cancellation was not possible, please request a return or stoppage of shipment, or other options, depending on the situation.

Change Your Subscribe & Save Delivery Day

After you place your order, you can change the delivery day of your subscriptions in Your Subscribe & Save.

To change your Subscribe & Save delivery day:

  1. Go to Your Subscribe & Save Items.
  2. Select the Settings tab.
  3. Select the delivery day below Monthly/Weekly subscriptions.
  4. Use the calendar to select your new delivery day.
  5. Select Confirm.

Your subscription change will take effect on your next delivery.

Change Your Subscribe & Save Delivery Schedule

After you place your order, you can change your delivery schedule or change the item quantity.

To change your Subscribe & Save subscription:

  1. Go to Your Subscribe & Save Items in Your Account.
  2. In the Subscriptions tab select the subscription for the item you want to change.
  3. Select Change your schedule or quantity.
  4. You can change the frequency, your next delivery date, or the quantity of items.
  5. Make your change.
  6. Select Confirm.

Your subscription change will take effect on your next delivery.

Learn About Comixology Unlimited

A subscription to Comixology Unlimited gives you access to borrow from a large selection of comics, graphic novels, and manga without due dates.

Comixology Unlimited allows you to read freely from thousands of titles, including comic books, graphic novels & manga. Comixology Unlimited is available for $5.99 a month and you can cancel anytime.

Use your Amazon account to borrow up to 50 eligible Comixology Unlimited titles at a time. Comixology Unlimited titles display a badge in the Kindle Store for easy identification. Access borrowed titles, online or offline, from your compatible Amazon device or Kindle reading app, even while traveling.

Note:

  • Currently, Comixology Unlimited is only available in the U.S.
  • Borrowed titles can be delivered to and accessed on Wi-Fi enabled Kindle devices or through the Kindle app, but they can’t be downloaded or transferred via USB.

If you haven’t tried Comixology Unlimited before, you can sign up for a free trial. At the end of your free trial, you’re automatically converted to a monthly paid subscription plan.

To use Comixology Unlimited, you must have an Amazon account with a current, valid payment method and you must have 1-Click enabled. To sign up for or manage your membership go to 0.

Titles in the Comixology Unlimited catalog can change at any time.

Learn About Kindle Unlimited

With a Kindle Unlimited subscription, you can borrow from our large collection of digital books, audiobooks, comics, manga, and magazines. Simply look for the Kindle Unlimited badge, download, and start reading.

Eligibility

If eligible, you can sign up for a free trial of Kindle Unlimited. At the end of your free trial, you’re automatically converted to a monthly paid subscription plan.

To use Kindle Unlimited, you must have an Amazon account with a current, valid payment method.

For more information on availability, go to: Kindle Unlimited Eligible Countries & Territories.

Using Your Subscription

You can access Kindle Unlimited on any Kindle device, smartphone, computer, or tablet through the free Kindle app.

Borrow up to 20 titles at a time from a wide selection of genres, all with no due dates. Magazine issues count toward this 20-title limit. When you want something new, return your current selections and dive right into fresh titles.

Tips for finding Kindle Unlimited titles:

  • You can find Kindle Unlimited books in both the Kindle Store and your library by turning on the Kindle Unlimited filter in your Kindle app.
  • To find Kindle Unlimited audiobooks, look for the “with Narration” (headset) icon on the Kindle book pages, or browse the Kindle Unlimited store and filter by audiobooks.
  • You can find Kindle Unlimited magazines in the Kindle store by turning on the Kindle Unlimited filter and then searching for “magazines” or specific titles. Borrow magazines in the Kindle for Android app, Amazon shopping app for Android, or your mobile or desktop browser to download your chosen magazine. At this time, you can’t borrow directly from the Amazon mobile shopping app for iOS or on your eReader. However, you can read downloaded magazines on the Kindle app on all your devices. Note: Starting April 22, 2026, each magazine issue you borrow through Kindle Unlimited counts toward your 20-title borrow limit. We also no longer automatically deliver new issues. Previously, you could borrow up to 50 magazine issues. By July 30, 2026, if you have more than 20 borrowed titles, we will automatically return the excess.

Kindle Unlimited members can share Kindle Unlimited content with Amazon Family members. To learn more about sharing content, visit Share Kindle Unlimited Content with Amazon Family Members.

Tip: You can also give a Kindle Unlimited subscription as a gift, choosing between 6-, 12-, and 24-month options. For more information, visit Kindle Unlimited Gifting.

Please note that titles in the Kindle Unlimited catalog can change at any time.

Managing Your Subscription

To sign up, manage, or cancel your subscription, visit Kindle Unlimited.

For help updating your payment method, go to How to Update Your Amazon Payment Method.

For help canceling your subscription, go to: Cancel Your Kindle Unlimited Subscription.

Learn About Prime Reading

Prime members can borrow up to 10 eligible titles at a time from a large selection of books, including books with Audible Narration, Amazon Originals, comics, and magazines.

Prime Reading offers a rotating catalog of Kindle Unlimited titles for you to choose from.

To find Prime Reading books, go to Prime Reading in your browser. When you find a title you want to borrow, select Add to Library.

When you want to return a Prime Reading title:

  1. Go to Manage Your Content and Devices.
  2. From the Your Content tab, use the drop-down menu to filter by Prime Reading.
  3. Select Return this title and confirm when prompted.

Managing Purchased Content in Your Library

Songs and albums you’ve purchased on Amazon are stored in your Library > Purchased view. This content is permanently tied to your Amazon account and available for playback at any time.

Can I Delete Purchased Songs from My Library?

The option to remove purchased songs from your Library > Purchased view is not currently available on any platform, including:

  • Android

  • iOS

  • Web Player

  • Download Management

    • What It Does: Remove downloaded songs from your device to free up storage. Path: Settings > Downloads > Remove Download Note: This does not delete the song from your Library .

  • Playback Control

    • What It Does: Purchased songs will not play automatically unless you select them directly or add them to a playlist.

  • Unlike

    • What It Does: If you Unlike (tap on filled heart), the song will be removed from Library songs, but will still be available on your purchased page.

Frequently Asked Questions

Will this feature be available in the future?

Our product team is tracking this and values your feedback. Your input plays a key role in shaping future improvements.

Can Customer Service remove purchased songs for me?

No. There is currently no process - including through Customer Service - to remove purchased songs from your Library.

Order a Subscribe & Save Subscription

Subscribe & Save offers item discounts, free standard shipping, and the convenience of automatic deliveries.

To order a Subscribe & Save subscription:

  1. Find a Subscribe & Save eligible item.
  2. Select the Subscribe & Save option, and select your preferred quantity and frequency.
  3. Select Set Up Now.
  4. Review your order details.
  5. Select Confirm Subscription. Note: You can only use credit cards and the Amazon.com Store Card to create Subscribe & Save subscriptions. You can apply available credit balances already on your account from Amazon.com Gift Cards by selecting the check box that says Use gift card balance if available. If you don’t select this check box during checkout, your available gift card funds won’t automatically apply. We’ll apply promotional credits on your account to existing subscriptions before your credit card is charged.

You can view your subscription under Your Subscribe & Save items in Your Account. We’ll also email you to confirm your subscription.

Before each shipment, we’ll email you to let you know your next order is being processed, so you can make any necessary updates.

Note:

  • We send Subscribe & Save orders using free standard shipping for auto-deliveries scheduled to arrive on your delivery day. If you have one-time purchase orders arriving near your delivery day, your auto-deliveries may arrive together.
  • Some items may be subject to quantity limits.

Redeem a Kindle Book Gift

Did you receive a Kindle book gift email? Accept your gift from the email notification to begin reading on your supported Amazon device or download a Kindle reading app.

Note: If you do not live in the same country as the person who gave you the Kindle book gift, the book may not be available to you due to copyright restrictions. In these cases, you are given the option to request an exchange for an Amazon.com Gift Card on your account during the redemption process.

  1. Select Redeem your gift from your gift notification email.
  2. Select Redeem now and if prompted, log-in to your Amazon account.
  3. From the drop-down menu, select the supported Amazon device you want your gift delivered to.

Redeem a Prepaid Kindle Book

When you receive a prepaid Kindle book, you can redeem it immediately and read on your compatible Amazon device or download a Kindle reading app.

Note: If you do not live in the same country as the person who sent you the prepaid Kindle book, the book will not be available to you due to country restrictions. The prepaid Kindle book may also be unavailable if someone else has already used the redemption link or if the person who sent you the book has requested a refund. In these cases, please contact the person who sent you the book to request another copy.

  1. If you received a notification email from the person who purchased the book, select the redemption link in the email. Your browser opens an Amazon page to accept the book. If you received the redemption link printed on a physical piece of paper, input the redemption link into the address bar of your browser.
  2. Sign in using your Amazon account details, then select Accept this eBook. Note: If you do not yet have an Amazon account, provide your information to create your Amazon account and click Accept this eBook.
  3. After accepting your book, you can immediately begin reading your book in the Kindle reading app. Your book also appears in the Cloud or Archived Items on any other Kindle devices or Kindle reading apps registered to your account.

Return a Kindle Book Order

Cancel an accidental book order within seven days.

Where a book has been partially read or in cases of abuse, refunds may be denied. Approved refunds are credited to the original payment source within three to five days.

Cancel an accidental book order within seven days

Start Your Kindle Book Return

  1. Go to Your Orders and sign in with the Amazon account you used to make the purchase.
  2. From the Digital Orders tab, select the Return for Refund button next to the title you want to return.
  3. In the pop-up window, choose the reason for return (optional), then select Return for Refund.

Note: If you have a high rate of return requests in your account history or you have partially read a book, the self-service refund option may not be available to you.

If you paid through Google Play Billing (Google Wallet):

  1. Go to the Google Play Store app.

  2. Select your profile icon.

  3. Select Payments & Subscriptions.

  4. Select Budget & Order history to find your order and request a refund. Remember: Approved refunds are credited to the Google Play payment method you used for the purchase.

    Tip: Prevent accidental purchases by setting parental controls.

Return Unredeemed Kindle Book Gift Orders

Request a refund for unredeemed Kindle books within 60 days of purchase.

Note: Orders delivered to the receiver or redeemed by the receiver are not eligible for return.

  1. Go to Your Orders and sign in with the same Amazon account information you used to purchase your content.
  2. Locate your order or title and select Return unredeemed books. All unredeemed copies will be returned.
  3. Select your reason for return, and then Submit.

Approved refunds are credited to the original payment method used to make the purchase and usually completed within 3-5 business days.

Share Kindle Unlimited Content with Amazon Family Members

Send borrowed Kindle Unlimited titles to members of your Amazon Family on their devices, or enable automatic sharing.

To set up an Amazon Family, go to Create Your Amazon Family.

To share borrowed content with a member of your Amazon Family:

  1. Select Share Your Kindle Content on the Home screen of the Kindle App.
  2. Select the Amazon Family member that you would like to share with.
  3. Select books that you would like to share.
  4. Select Share Items. To turn on automatic sharing, follow steps 1 and 2, and then select Share Future Purchases and Kindle Unlimited titles by Default. Turning on automatic sharing shares future borrows and purchases.

Note: Only the primary Kindle Unlimited account holder can initiate borrows.

Skip Your Next Subscribe & Save Delivery

You can choose to skip your next delivery in Your Subscribe & Save Items.

To skip one item in your next Subscribe & Save delivery:

  1. Go to Your Subscribe & Save Items.
  2. Hover over the subscription for the item you want to change.
  3. Select Skip.
  4. Confirm Skip until next delivery.

To skip an entire delivery for your next Subscribe & Save delivery:

  1. Go to Manage your Deliveries.
  2. Select Skip All.
  3. Confirm the items you wish to skip until next delivery.

Subscribe & Save (With Subscribe & Save, you’ll enjoy free standard)

With Subscribe & Save, you’ll enjoy free standard shipping on auto-deliveries (non-Prime customers may incur shipping costs for the first delivery), and the option to schedule deliveries on your Subscribe & Save delivery day. Eligible products often come at a discounted price on the Amazon price. You can cancel your subscription at any time.

To find eligible products, browse our Subscribe & Save store or search for Subscribe & Save followed by the product you’re interested in.

If there’s a discount for the item, it displays on the product detail page when you sign up. This discount may change at any time. If an item has already shipped before the discount changes, you’ll receive the old discount on that order. The new discount, if any, will apply to future orders.

Unlock extra savings when receiving five subscriptions in a delivery to a single address with auto-deliveries.

Note:

  • The item price and any applicable discount for your order is calculated before your reminder email. The price of the item may decrease or increase between deliveries, but will never be higher than the price shown in your reminder email.
  • You can’t pay for Subscribe & Save subscriptions with an Electronic Benefits Transfer (EBT) card for the Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP), because a PIN identification is required before each purchase.

Documents and Send to Kindle

Access Your Notes Added to Books and Personal Documents

In the consolidated view, you can access all of your highlights, underlines, handwritten notes, and text notes.

To access all of your notes and highlights for a book or document:

  • Tap on the top of the screen to open the main toolbar and tap on the notebook icon, or follow these steps:
  1. Tap on any sticky note icon inside the book or document.
  2. Tap on the 3-dot menu, and select All notes.

Tapping on any item in the consolidated view takes you to that note’s location in your book or document.

On Kindle Scribe models released in 2025 and later, annotations now have timestamps for simpler sorting. Sorting options include date created, date modified, and viewing annotations in ascending or descending order. The annotation notebook updates instantly to reflect your new choices.

  • Share Notes and Notebooks from Your Kindle Scribe
  • Delete Notes Added to Books and Personal Documents

Add an Email Address to Receive Documents in Your Kindle Library

You only receive documents from email addresses that you’ve added to your Approved Personal Document Email List in Manage Your Content and Devices.

Need to update Personal Document Settings? This and many other options are available on:

Manage Your Content and Devices

  1. Go to Manage Your Content and Devices.
  2. From Preferences scroll down to Personal Document Settings.
  3. Select Approved Personal Document Email List, and check for your email address. If you didn’t find your email address, select Add a new e-mail address.
  4. Enter the desired email address, make sure it’s complete and valid, and then select Add Address.

Connect, Browse, and Transfer Files on E-Reader

Use a USB cable to connect your Kindle and choose between browsing and transferring files to and from your computer, or charging only.

Connect Your Kindle E-Reader for Mac Users

To transfer files to Kindle, we recommend using our wireless Send-to-Kindle service (Send to Kindle), which can securely transfer files up to 200 MB each. Supported file types include: PDF, DOC, DOCX, TXT, RTF, HTM, HTML, PNG, GIF, JPG, JPEG, BMP, EPUB. If you prefer to use a USB cable to transfer files, Kindle Scribe and 2024-released Kindle devices require a separate application, such as our Send to Kindle app for Mac OS, which now supports USB file transfers. The USB Manager feature works with Intel and Mac M1-M3 computers with MacOS 12 and above. To install the app, follow the instructions below.

If you prefer to use a USB cable to transfer files, Kindle Scribe and 2024-released Kindle devices require a separate application, such as our Send to Kindle app for Mac OS, which now supports USB file transfers. The USB Manager feature works with Intel and Mac M1-M3 computers with MacOS 12 and above. To install the app, follow the instructions below.

Note: Mac computers before OSX 10.5 or Kindle device models released earlier than 2024 (except for Kindle Scribe) don’t require a separate application to transfer files. To determine which Kindle e-reader model you’re using, visit our page: Identify Your Kindle E-Reader.

Send-to-Kindle for Mac Instructions:

To install the Send-to-Kindle app, download the file using this link: SendToKindleForMac-installer.pkg. By using Send to Kindle, you agree to the terms in Send to Kindle Legal Information.

To connect your Kindle to your Mac and browse or transfer files:

  1. Use the USB cable that came with your Kindle and plug it to the USB port on your computer.
  2. Open Send-to-Kindle app.
    • Access the USB File Manager feature on the top-left of the screen under Tools > USB File Manager.
    • Alternatively, after logging in with your Amazon account, click “Open USB File Manager” on the top right of the Send-to-Kindle window.
  4. A separate window will appear allowing you to browse your Kindle’s files and folders.
  5. Drag and drop files/folders between your Mac computer and the “Documents” folder on your Kindle.

To delete or rename files on the Kindle, right-click or two-finger tap on the file and select “Delete“ or “Rename”. You can also delete by selecting a file and pressing Command + Delete on the keyboard.

Try these troubleshooting tips if you experience issues when transferring files on Mac:

  • If the Kindle isn’t recognized on your computer when connecting a USB cable, try the following: - Close other USB file transfer applications. - Click the “Connect device” button on the USB File Manager window. - Unplug and replug the USB cable to the USB port on your computer.
  • The USB File Manager only supports drag and drop at this time.
  • USB transfers of more than 2,000 files at a time are not supported.

Note: There are also various third-party software options available for transferring files to a Mac computer, however, Amazon is unable to verify or guarantee the security, functionality, or reliability of these third-party options.

Connect Your Kindle E-reader for Windows Users

You don’t require a separate application to connect and transfer files on Windows computers. Kindle devices appear as an external storage drive on the computer’s desktop when you first connect them via USB. The Kindle device shows up as “Kindle” or “Kindle Scribe” in File Explorer instead of a drive letter.

Try these troubleshooting tips if you experience issues when transferring files:

  • If an error occurs or the Kindle isn’t recognized, unplug and replug the USB cable to the USB port on your computer.
  • To transfer a file between your computer and your Kindle successfully, the entire file path shouldn’t exceed 256 characters.
  • Any renaming, compressing, or extracting of the file needs to occur on the computer and not within the Kindle file system.

Note: There’s no need to select an eject button on the Mac or Windows computer. To eject the Kindle, select the Disconnect button on the Kindle screen or unplug the USB cable from the computer.

Convert Handwritten Notes to Text on Kindle Scribe

Add converted notes to other documents or share them via email from your device.

To access the Convert to text options in the Share menu:

  1. Go to the Notebook tab.
  2. Pick a downloaded notebook and select the More menu under the notebook’s thumbnail.
  3. Select Share and choose one of these options:

    • Convert to text and quick send [to your registered email].

    • Convert to text and email. A preview window opens and you can edit the text by tapping on each page.

You can also access these options by tapping on the Share icon at the top of a notebook.

Note: Edits made to the converted text aren’t saved on your device.

To change the handwriting recognition language, go to Settings > Device Options > Language & Dictionaries > Handwriting Recognition. The change applies to all notebook conversions to text.

Note: Currently, this feature supports the following handwriting recognition languages: Afrikaans, Arabic, Chinese (Simplified), Chinese (Traditional) - HK, Chinese (Traditional) - TW, Danish, Dutch, English (UK), English (U.S.), French, French (Canada), German, Icelandic, Italian, Japanese, Norwegian, Portuguese (Brazil), Russian, Spanish, and Swedish.

To add converted notes to other documents, copy/paste them from the shared .txt file sent to the email addresses that you specified.

  • Share Notes and Notebooks from Your Kindle Scribe
  • Fix Issues with Handwriting Recognition on Kindle Scribe

Delete Notes Added to Books and Personal Documents

Learn how to delete an active canvas or a sticky note.

To delete an active canvas while in the book:

  1. Touch the canvas frame with the pointer and select the delete icon on the bottom-right corner.
  2. Confirm the deletion on the dialogue that opens next.

To delete a sticky note while in the book:

  1. Select the note icon to open it.
  2. Select the 3-dot menu, and then Delete.

Alternatively, from the consolidated view of your notes for the book, select the 3-dot menu for that note or canvas, then select Delete.

  • View Your Notes Added to Books and Personal Documents
  • Share Notes and Notebooks from Your Kindle Scribe

Disable Document Archiving in Your Kindle Library

To stop automatic storage of documents in Manage Your Content and Devices, disable automatic document archiving.

Need to update Personal Document Settings? This and many other options are available on: Manage Your Content and Devices

  1. Go to Manage Your Content and Devices.
  2. Select the Preferences tab, and then scroll to Personal Document Settings.
  3. Under Personal Document Archiving, select Edit Archive Settings.
  4. Uncheck the box next to Enable personal document archiving to my Kindle library and select Update.

Edit Your Send to Kindle Email Address

Customize your Kindle email address, used with the Kindle Personal Document Service, to make it more secure.

Need to update Personal Document Settings? This and many other options are available on: Manage Your Content and Devices

  1. Go to Manage Your Content and Devices.
  2. From Preferences scroll down to Personal Document Settings.
  3. Under Send to Kindle Email Settings, your Send to Kindle email address is listed for your compatible device or app.
  4. To change this email address select Edit next to your device, enter a new email address, and then select Save to save your changes.

Import Personal Documents to Kindle for Windows

Import supported personal documents from your PC.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  1. In the Kindle for Windows app left menu, select Library, then select Import.
  2. From the screen that pops up, find the document that you wish to import to Kindle. Remember: Supported file types are PDF, DOC, DOCX, TXT, RTF, HTM, HTML, PNG, GIF, JPG, JPEG, BMP, and EPUB.
  3. Import the personal document by selecting Open, then follow the prompts. Tip: Alternatively, you can drag and drop files into your Kindle for Windows app library.
  4. Sync your library to view your imported content. Your Kindle library may take a few moments to show the imported documents.

For more on how Amazon handles your information, go to Privacy Notice and Kindle Store Terms of Use.

Kindle Personal Document File Types That Support Notes

Discover which Send to Kindle file types are compatible with Kindle formats and notes for your Kindle E-Reader and Kindle applications.

Note: You can write directly on the page in PDF documents, or write on the page in other personal documents imported through the Send to Kindle service. Kindle Scribe doesn’t currently support writing directly on the page in PDF documents loaded via USB-C or previously sent to your library before 11/11/2022.

Kindle Scribe doesn’t support handwriting features in EPUB comics, Manga, and children’s books currently.

  • Kindle Scribe
    • DOC, DOCX, TXT, RTF, HTM, HTML, PNG, GIF, JPG, JPEG, BMP, EPUB: Handwritten sticky notes Text notes Active canvas Highlights Underlines
    • PDF (adjustable layout): Handwritten sticky notes Text notes Active canvas Highlights Underlines
  • Other Kindle E-readers
    • DOC, DOCX, TXT, RTF, HTM, HTML, PNG, GIF, JPG, JPEG, BMP, EPUB: Highlights Text notes
    • PDF (adjustable layout): Highlights Text notes
  • Kindle for iOS
    • DOC, DOCX, TXT, RTF, HTM, HTML, PNG, GIF, JPG, JPEG, BMP, EPUB: Handwritten sticky notes (read only) Text notes Highlights
    • PDF (adjustable layout): Handwritten sticky notes (read only) Text notes Highlights
  • Kindle for Android Kindle app on Fire Tablet
    • DOC, DOCX, TXT, RTF, HTM, HTML, PNG, GIF, JPG, JPEG, BMP, EPUB: Handwritten sticky notes (read only) Text notes Highlights
    • PDF (adjustable layout): Handwritten sticky notes (read only) Text notes Highlights
  • Kindle for Mac Kindle for PC
    • DOC, DOCX, TXT, RTF, HTM, HTML, PNG, GIF, JPG, JPEG, BMP, EPUB: Text notes Highlights
    • PDF (adjustable layout): Text notes Highlights
  • Kindle for Web
    • DOC, DOCX, TXT, RTF, HTM, HTML, PNG, GIF, JPG, JPEG, BMP, EPUB: N/A (personal documents aren’t supported yet)
    • PDF (adjustable layout): N/A (personal documents aren’t supported yet)
