Learn About Sending Documents to Your Kindle Library

The Send to Kindle service allows you to send documents to your Kindle library on your Kindle devices and Kindle app at no additional cost. You can upload documents through the web, an authorized email address, the Kindle app for iOS and Android devices, or a Send to Kindle application if available. All documents sent through Send to Kindle are protected with end to end encryption.

Send to Kindle for Web

Upload documents (200 MB or smaller) directly from your device with Send to Kindle for Web. Make sure to turn on Add to your library so that you can download documents in your library to any device linked to your account.

Use Send to Kindle for Web to upload documents to your device. Send to Kindle

Send to Kindle from the Kindle App for iOS and Android Devices

Send documents to your Kindle library from your iOS or Android phone or tablet using the free Kindle app for iOS and Android.

  • Send to Kindle iOS
  • Send to Kindle Android

Send to Kindle for Email

Send your file(s) as an attachment to your Send to Kindle email address. Your Send to Kindle email address is a unique address assigned to each compatible device and Kindle app on your account.

Send to Kindle Desktop Applications

Send documents from your desktop directly to your Kindle library, using the Send to Kindle app for Mac and Windows.

  • Send to Kindle Mac
  • Send to Kindle Windows

Send to Kindle Chrome Extension

Send web content like articles, blog posts, and more, to your Kindle library using the Send to Kindle Chrome browser extension.

Send to Kindle - Supported File Formats

Send to Kindle supports the following file types:

  • HTML (.HTML, .HTM)

  • RTF (.RTF)

  • Text (.TXT)

  • JPEG (.JPEG, .JPG)

  • GIF (.GIF)

  • PNG (.PNG)

  • BMP (.BMP)

  • PDF (.PDF)

  • EPUB (.EPUB)

Learn How to Use Your Send to Kindle Email Address

Send documents directly to your Kindle Library through Send to Kindle for Web, by email or with the Send to Kindle app if available.

With your Send to Kindle email address, you can:

  • Send up to 25 attachments in one email.
  • Send to your Send to Kindle email from up to 15 approved email addresses.
  • Send a total size of 50 MB or less. Compress your documents into a ZIP file to send more than 50 MB. The conversion service automatically opens the ZIP files and converts them to the Kindle format.

For your email to be delivered successfully:

  • Add the sender’s email address to your Approved Personal Document Email List on the Manage Your Content and Devices page.

  • Verify that the document is a supported file type.

  • Attach the document to your email.

  • Be sure that your device is connected to the internet. The delivery for your documents will be attempted for up to 60 days.

Manage Your Kindle Scribe Content and Documents Using Workspace

The new Workspace tab on Kindle Scribe allows you to organize your Library content alongside notebooks.

Note: To use these features on your Kindle Scribe:

  • You need a Kindle Scribe released in 2025 or later.
  • You need to register your Kindle Scribe to an Amazon account and connect it to the internet.

You can now have books, documents and notebooks in one same folder.

To Add Items to Workspace While in Library:

  1. In your Library, locate the item that you want to add and long-press on it.
  2. Select Add to Workspace.
  3. Choose the specific location where you want it and tap Add.

To Add Items from Your Library While in Workspace:

  1. In Workspace, tap on the tile with the large plus icon .
    • Choose Add book or document and select items from your Library. Use search, sort, and filter options to locate items.
    • From this step, you can also choose to Create notebook or Create folder.
  3. Tap Add.

To Move or Remove Workspace Items:

Note: You can’t remove notebooks and folders from Workspace.

  1. Long-press on the item that you want to move.
  2. Select Move/remove from Workspace in the bottom menu.
    • Choose the folder where you want the item or navigate to the root Workspace folder and tap Move.
    • Or tap Remove from Workspace.

Library items that you remove from Workspace are still available in your Library.

Remove Personal Documents from Kindle for Windows

Delete local files and cloud documents to clear space on your local library.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  1. Select the Content tab on the top left.
  2. Select View on the top left, then select Docs.
  3. Find the document you wish to remove, then select Deliver or remove from device.
  4. Uncheck the Kindle for Windows app, then select Make Changes.

Tips about Converting Personal Documents to a Kindle Format

Are you unable to insert an active canvas or a sticky note, create on-page markup in your documents in the Kindle library, or are you receiving conversion fail errors?

Note:

  • Print format/fix layout documents (PDFs) support writing directly on the page with Kindle Scribe.
  • Adjustable layout documents (Microsoft word, txt, and the like) support on-page writing with active canvas and sticky notes on Kindle Scribe.

If you’re receiving conversion fail errors:

  • Remove any password protection or security settings in your document before sending it to your Kindle library.
  • Delete any Microsoft Word ink drawings from your .doc or .docx document. Ink drawings will cause the document conversion to fail.
  • Make sure that your document is in one of the following supported languages: Afrikaans, Alsatian, Arabic, Basque, Bokmål Norwegian, Breton, Catalan, Chinese (Simplified), Cornish, Corsican, Danish, Dutch, Eastern Frisian, English, Finnish, French, Frisian, Galician, German, Icelandic, Irish, Italian, Luxembourgish, Manx, Northern Frisian, Norwegian, Nynorsk Norwegian, Portuguese, Provencal, Romansh, Russian, Scots, Scottish Gaelic, Spanish, Swahili, Swedish, Welsh, and the Indic languages Malayalam, Tamil, Hindi, Gujarati, and Marathi.
  • Remove any large tables or tables with complex format tables.

If you’re unable to insert an active canvas or a sticky note, or create on-page markups in your documents on Kindle Scribe:

  • Try sending the document again through Send to Kindle to make sure it has the latest features available for Kindle Scribe.
  • If documents contain elements that are currently not supported, on-page mark-ups, active canvas, or sticky note inserts won’t work on Kindle Scribe. Handwritten annotations are currently not supported on documents containing:
    • Tables: Including tables with both horizontal and vertical text, larger in size, ones with drop-caps, and tables within tables.
    • Multi-Media content: Audio, video, and SVG images
    • Mathematical equations
    • Footnotes inside footnotes
    • Linear Gradients

Documents sent to your Kindle Apps and devices before 11/11/2022 are not compatible with the latest Kindle features. To activate these features, please import through one of the Send to Kindle service options.

If you added your document to Kindle library after 11/11/2022 and don’t have the latest Kindle features available:

Please try one of the following tips to receive the latest Kindle format:

  • Remove download through the book action menu and re-download the document. - If the “Remove download” menu option isn’t available, but the “Download” option is instead, the document may not have been downloaded before. Tap “Download” to download the document. - If you the “Remove Download” or “Download” options are not available, try importing the document again.

  • Send the documents again through one of the Send to Kindle service options.

If you’re sending documents directly from the Microsoft Word app from Windows, Mac, or Web:

Here are some helpful tips about how your Word documents will look like on Kindle:

  • Tracked changes are automatically accepted, regardless of which option you choose.
  • Highlights are displayed on Kindle but cannot be edited.
  • Comments:
    • When you select “Like a Kindle book”, comments in your Word documents are automatically converted into Kindle footnotes with a gray background by design.
    • When you select “Like a printed documents”, comments in your Word documents are automatically hidden.
  • Ink drawings:
    • When you select “Like a Kindle book” for your .docx files in the Word for Windows and Mac, ink drawings are automatically removed such that your document can be converted to a Kindle format.
      • Please delete any Microsoft Word ink drawings from your .doc and .docx document. Ink drawings in .doc and .docx documents will cause the document conversion to fail.
    • When you select “Like a printed documents”, ink drawings on top of the page are preserved in the document.
  • Kindle Personal Documents File Types That Support Notes
  • Learn About Sending Documents to Your Kindle Library (including supported file types)

Troubleshoot Send to Kindle Errors

To troubleshoot your Send to Kindle errors, find your error code in the list and then follow the steps provided.

  • E001
    • Error Description: Unsupported File Format
    • Troubleshooting Steps: Send to Kindle does not support this file format. Please find the supported file formats on the Learn About Sending Documents to Your Kindle Library page. If the file format is supported, please make sure that the document is not password protected or encrypted, and try sending it again.
  • E002
    • Error Description: Invalid or Corrupted MOBI File
    • Troubleshooting Steps: The MOBI file sent is either invalid or corrupted. Please ensure that the document is not password protected or encrypted, and try sending it again.
  • E004
    • Error Description: Password Protected File
    • Troubleshooting Steps: Send to Kindle does not support password protected and other encrypted files. Please ensure that the document is not password protected or encrypted, and try sending it again.
  • E005
    • Error Description: Corrupted PDF File
    • Troubleshooting Steps: The PDF file is either encrypted or corrupted. Please make sure of the following and try sending the file again: The document is not password protected or encrypted. The document can be opened using PDF reader applications.
  • E006
    • Error Description: Attachment Count Limit Exceeded
    • Troubleshooting Steps: Too many files have been attached to the sent email. Please ensure that the email does not contain more than 25 documents (before compression if zipped), and try sending the email again or split them into different emails.
  • E007
    • Error Description: Attachment Size Limit Exceeded
    • Troubleshooting Steps: One or more files attached to the sent email exceeds the limit. Please ensure that each attachment does not exceed 50 MB in size (before compression if zipped), and try sending the email again or split them into different emails.
  • E008
    • Error Description: Recipient Limit Exceeded
    • Troubleshooting Steps: Too many recipients were added to the email. Please ensure that the email does not contain more than 15 recipients, and try sending the email again. As a reminder, you may only use Send to Kindle for your personal, non-commercial use. By using Send to Kindle, you agree to the Send to Kindle Terms of Use .
  • E009
    • Error Description: No Attachment
    • Troubleshooting Steps: The sent email does not include any attachment(s). Please attach the document(s) and try sending the email again.
  • E010
    • Error Description: Email Throttle Warning
    • Troubleshooting Steps: Too many emails are being sent from this email address. As a reminder, you may only use Send to Kindle for your personal, non-commercial use. By using Send to Kindle, you agree to the Send to Kindle Terms of Use .
  • E011
    • Error Description: Email Throttle Final Warning
    • Troubleshooting Steps: Too many emails are being sent from this email address, continuously. Kindle has initiated a review of your Send to Kindle usage. If you are found to be in violation of our Send to Kindle Terms of Use , your use of Send to Kindle will be suspended.
  • E012
    • Error Description: Email Throttle Error
    • Troubleshooting Steps: Too many emails were being sent from this email address, continuously. We have suspended your use of Send to Kindle for suspected violation of our Send to Kindle Terms of Use , including the restriction on using the Send to Kindle service for personal, non-commercial use only. While you are suspended, documents you try to send from this email address to Kindle will not be delivered. Your intended Kindle recipients will be notified to contact you for the unfulfilled documents.
  • E013
    • Error Description: Incompatible Elements for Kindle Format Conversion
    • Troubleshooting Steps: The document sent contains incompatible elements for Kindle format conversion. We delivered the document so that you can read it on Kindle, but in its current format it won’t support the latest Kindle features for documents, including Kindle Scribe handwriting features. Please ensure the following to make the document compatible with Kindle format conversion, and try sending it again. Split large tables into several smaller tables, align the direction of text in the tables, and remove drop-caps in the tables. Remove multimedia content such as audio, video, and SVG images. Remove multimedia content such as audio, video, and SVG images. Remove mathematical equations. Remove images that contain linear gradients. Remove password protection or any other security settings. Remove any footnotes inside footnotes. Check the document can be opened on a computer. Make sure the document doesn’t have more than 8,000 pages.
  • E014
    • Error Description: Unapproved Sender Email Address
    • Troubleshooting Steps: Someone tried to send document(s) to your Kindle. Since the sender is not in your Kindle approved e-mail list, the document(s) sent to you will not be delivered to your Kindle. To approve the sender’s e-mail address: Visit Manage Your Content and Devices page. Sign in to your Amazon account. Go to Personal Document Settings under Manage Your Content and Devices . Go to Approved Personal Document Email List and select Add a new approved email address . Enter the email address to approve and select Add Address . Then, instruct the sender to resend the document(s). Important: Remember to only add email addresses as approved contacts if they belong to sources you trust. Please note that you will receive this notification only once. In the future, only documents sent from approved email addresses will be delivered to you.
  • E999
    • Error Description: Send to Kindle Internal Error
    • Troubleshooting Steps: The document(s) could not be delivered due to an internal error. Please try sending your document(s) again in some time.
    • Error Description: Service Under Maintenance
    • Troubleshooting Steps: The Send to Kindle service is currently under maintenance. Please try sending your document(s) again in some time.
  • E015
    • Error Description: Web Extraction Error
    • Troubleshooting Steps: A webpage (HTML file) sent via Send to Kindle could not be delivered due to content extraction errors. This typically occurs when the webpage is empty, protected, or contains incompatible elements. Please verify the webpage displays correctly on your device, then try sending it again.

Use Your Pen to Handwrite on Books and Personal Documents

Add an active canvas or a sticky note and write using the writing toolbar. Use writing tools such as Pen, Highlighter, Eraser, and Undo/Redo. Double tap on Pen, Highlighter, or Eraser to access custom options for those tools.

The toolbar options also allow you to place the toolbar on the left or right side of the screen.

To hide the writing toolbar across all books and documents:

  1. To display the top navigation bar, tap on the top of the screen.
  2. Tap on the 3-dot icon, and select Hide Writing Toolbar.

To show the toolbar again, go to the same 3-dot menu and select Show Writing Toolbar.

In books and compatible Personal Documents, you’re able to create active canvases or sticky notes:

To insert an active canvas:

  1. Select the Active Canvas tool in the writing toolbar.
  2. Press and hold between book lines to insert the active canvas.

The active canvas inserted between lines is as wide as the text column.

You can also create a custom-size canvas simply by creating a stroke on the page. A translucent box shows with confirm or cancel buttons for insertion. The canvas keeps expanding as you keep writing before you confirm insertion.

To change a canvas size, touch the canvas frame with the pointer tool and pull the sides in or out.

To delete a canvas, touch the canvas frame with the pointer and select the trash icon on the bottom-right corner. Confirm the deletion on the dialogue that opens next.

Note: Active canvas is currently only available on Kindle Scribe, and only supports left-to-right horizontal languages. Right-to-left horizontal languages, such as Arabic, or vertical languages like Japanese, aren’t supported at this time.

To insert a sticky note:

  • Select the Note option in the annotation menu that appears when you tap on a highlight or underlined text, or
  • Select the sticky note tool in the writing toolbar, and then touch the page to place your note where you want it. A note icon shows marking where you saved your sticky note. Tap on the icon to reopen the note and make any edits.
  • If you create notes in the expandable margin, they also appear in sticky notes when the margin is closed.

Your sticky notes appear in the expandable margin when the margin is open.

Tip: To view or hide the action menu after highlighting or underlining text, activate or deactivate the Annotation Menu. Select the Aa menu in the top navigation bar, and then the More tab.

Notes get autosaved after you finish writing on your sticky note canvas and tap on the X to close it.

In certain books or PDFs that you import onto your device using the Send to Kindle service, you’re able to write notes directly on the page.

Note: The use of 3P covers may interfere with some of the screen writing areas.

  • Kindle Personal Document File Types That Support Notes
  • View Your Notes Added to Books and Personal Documents
  • Write in the Expandable Margin of eBooks on Kindle Scribe

Kindle apps on phones, tablets and computers

Add Items to Collections in Kindle for Windows

How to add books or documents to your Collections.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  1. Open the Kindle for Windows app.
  2. Select one or more items.
  3. Select the collections where you want to add or remove this item.
  4. Select Confirm.

Add or Remove Books from Your Kindle for Windows App

Add or remove a book to manage space on your home screen library.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  1. Open the Kindle app for Windows.
  2. Go to Manage Your Content And Devices.
  3. Select the Content tab on the top left.
  4. Select Books. For Kindle audiobooks, select Audiobook category.
  5. Find the book you wish to add or remove, then select Deliver or remove from device .
  6. Check or uncheck the Kindle for Windows app, then select Make Changes.

Copy Collections to the New Kindle for Mac App

Are you trying to copy over your local collections from the Kindle Classic app? Follow these steps:

  1. Go to the Kindle Classic app listing to get the latest update.
  2. Select Update to update the Kindle Classic app.
  3. Select Open to open the Kindle Classic app.
  4. Go to the new app listing and download the new Kindle for Mac App.
  5. Select Get to download and install the new Kindle for Mac App.
  6. Log in to the new Kindle for Mac, and you’ll find a pop-up prompting you to copy local collections from the old app.
  7. Collections will be immediately available when you download and sign in to the new Kindle for Mac App.

Create a Collection Cloud Copy on the Legacy Kindle App

Copy Local Collections created in the Legacy Kindle App to your account to sync on your other Kindle apps and devices.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  1. Kindle no longer supports local collections. To access your historical local collections from the Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC), create a copy and upload them to the cloud.
  2. Connect your PC to the internet.
  3. In the Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) home screen, below the Local Collection, select the three dots icon.
  4. Click Create a cloud copy.

Note: Sideloaded documents that are locally available won’t be added to the new cloud collection.

Create Collections in Kindle for Windows

How to create Collections to keep your Kindle library organized.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  1. Open the Kindle app for Windows
  2. Select + New Collection under Collections.
  3. Enter a name for your Collection, then select Create. Tip: You can also create a collection for by selecting one or more books, then select Create Collection

Delete Collections in Kindle for Windows

How to delete Collections from your Kindle library.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  1. In the Kindle for Windows app home screen, right-click on the name of the collection you wish to delete.
  2. Select Delete.
  3. Select Yes to confirm that you want to delete the collection.

Note: This action only deletes the Collection from all your Kindle apps and devices. The content remains available in your library.

Deregister Your Android Kindle App

To troubleshoot a problem or access a different library, deregister an Amazon account from your Kindle app.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select More from the navigation bar.
  3. Go to Settings (gear icon on top right).
  4. Select Sign Out.
  5. Select Sign Out to confirm that you want to deregister the Amazon account.

Deregister Your iOS Kindle App

To troubleshoot a problem or access a different library, deregister an Amazon account from your Kindle app.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select More from the navigation bar.
  3. Select Settings (gear icon on top right) and then Sign Out.
  4. Select Sign Out to confirm that you want to remove the Amazon account.

Deregister Your Kindle for Mac App

To troubleshoot a problem or access a different library, deregister an Amazon account from your Kindle app.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select More from the navigation bar.
  3. Select Settings.
  4. Under Registration is the name of the registered account. Next to the name select Update.
  5. Select Deregister this Kindle.

Deregister Your Kindle for Windows App

To troubleshoot a problem or access a different library, deregister an Amazon account from your Kindle app.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select the Settings icon from the top menu toolbar.
  3. Select Sign out, then confirm. 1. Note: Signing out will delete all downloaded content and deregister your device from the Kindle for Windows app

Download All Content in a Collection for Kindle for Windows

Bulk download the contents of a Collection for offline reading.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  1. Open the Kindle for Windows app.
  2. On the home screen, right click on the name of the collection.
  3. Select Download.

Download Books to Your Kindle App

After you’ve purchased your book, you can download and read it on the Kindle apps for PC, Mac, iOS, and Android. You can read content online with the Kindle for Web app.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Go to your Library.
  3. If you’re on a computer, double-click on the book cover. If you’re on a mobile device, select the book cover.
  4. A progress bar updates as the book downloads.

Your book opens after the download is complete.

If the book doesn’t open after download, double-click or select it once more.

  • Kindle Books Aren’t Showing in Your Library

Editing Local Collections in the New Kindle for Mac App

Are you trying to edit local collections in the new Kindle for Mac App?

Editing a collection is only enabled for synced collections. Follow these steps to sync your collection and enable editing:

  1. Open the local collection that you’re trying to sync.
  2. Select Sync to account on the banner at the top of the collection window.

Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop

Use the Kindle app to start reading from your Windows or Mac.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026.

Supported Operating Systems:

  • Windows: Windows 11.
  • Mac: OS x 10.14 or higher

Note: If your operating system isn’t supported, use Kindle for Web.

To download the Kindle app on desktop:

  1. Go to Download Kindle Apps.
  2. Select the appropriate download link for your device.
  3. Follow the on-screen installation instructions.

Install or Update the Kindle App on Android

Use the Kindle App to start reading from your devices.

Note: The Kindle for Android app runs on Android OS version 10.0 or higher.

  1. In your preferred Android app store, search “Kindle.”
  2. In the search results, select Kindle.
  3. Select Install to download and install, or Update to update the Kindle app.

Install or Update the Kindle App on iOS

Use the Kindle app to start reading from your devices.

Note: The Kindle for iOS app runs on iOS version 17.0 or higher.

  1. Select the App Store.
  2. Search “Kindle.”
  3. From the search results, select Kindle.
  4. Select Get to download and install, or Update to update the Kindle app.

Install or Update the Kindle for Mac App

Use the Kindle app to start reading from your devices.

  1. Select the App Store.
  2. Search “Kindle.”
  3. From the search results, select Kindle.
  4. Select Get to download and install, or Update to update the Kindle app.

Kindle for Mac Keyboard Shortcuts

You can use many keyboard shortcuts to navigate the Kindle for Mac application.

Anywhere in the Application

  • Open Library - Command, ALT, L

  • Sync - Command R

  • Kindle for Mac: Preferences - Command ,

  • Kindle for Mac: Quit Kindle for Mac - Command Q

  • File: Close Book - Command W

  • Actions: Sync and Check for New Items - Command R

  • Go: Sync to Furthest Page Read - Command R

  • Move the focus of the book above the current selection - Up Arrow

  • Move the focus of the book to the right of the current selection - Right Arrow

  • Move the focus of the book to the left of the current selection - Left Arrow

  • Move the focus of the book below the current selection - Down Arrow

  • Scroll the list of books up one page - Page Up, Command, Up Arrow

  • Scroll the list of books down one page - Page Down, Command, Down Arrow

  • Open selected book (go to last page read) - Return, Command, O

  • Next Page - Page Down, Command Down Arrow, Enter, Right Arrow, Space, or Down Arrow

  • Previous Page - Shift Enter, Shift Space, Page Up, Command Up Arrow, Left Arrow, or Up Arrow

  • Back - Delete, Backspace

  • Increase font size - Command +

  • Decrease font size - Command -

  • Bookmark - Command D

  • Open/Close Notes and Marks - Command B

  • Go to Location - Command G

  • Search - Command F

  • Open Flashcard side panel - Command, Shift, F

  • Open and close X-Ray for Textbooks - Command Y (only works on eligible Textbooks)

  • Export to Flashcards from Notebook - Command, Shift, N (when using the Notebook)

  • Export Notes from Notebook - Command, Alt, E (when using the Notebook)

Text-to-Speech is only supported with Mac OSX 10.10 or above.

To use these shortcuts, you must enable Text-to-Speech first. Press Command T or select the Tools menu and choose Start Text-to-Speech.

  • Toggle Text-to-Speech - Command T
  • Pause or resume reading - Space bar (while in a book)
  • Read the current sentence - Command S
  • Read the previous sentence - Command, Shift, Up arrow
  • Skip forward one sentence - Command, Shift, Down arrow
  • Increase speech rate - Shift +
  • Decrease speech rate - Shift -
  • Toggle continuous reading - Command, Shift, C
  • Text-to-Speech Read Instructions - Command I

Note: This feature only works on eligible Kindle content. You can configure the Text-to-Speech language from the System Preferences on your computer.

Kindle for Windows Keyboard Shortcuts

You can use many keyboard shortcuts to navigate the Kindle for Windows application.

Anywhere in the Application

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  • Exit the Application - ALT, F4
  • Sync - F5
  • Help - CTRL, F1
  • Keyboard navigation (begin and next element) - Tab
  • Keyboard navigation (previous element) - Shift, Tab

From Library

  • Move the focus to the book above the current selection - Up Arrow
  • Move the focus of the book to the right of the current selection - Right Arrow
  • Move the focus of the book to the left of the current selection - Left Arrow
  • Move the focus of the book below the current selection - Down Arrow
  • Right-click or open the context menu of the selected book - Shift, F9
  • Open the context menu of the selected book - Shift, F10
  • Scroll the list of books up one page (but do not change the current focus) - Page Up

While Reading a Book

  • Next Page - Page Down or Right Arrow or Down Arrow
  • Previous Page - Page Up or Left Arrow or Up Arrow
  • Back - Backspace
  • Increase Font Size - CTRL, + (doesn’t work on comics or content for children)
  • Decrease Font Size - CTRL, - (doesn’t work on comics or content for children)
  • Bookmark - CTRL, D
  • Open Annotation Notebook (i.e. Notes) Highlights, if closed - CTRL, N
  • Close Annotation Notebook (i.e. Notes) Highlights, if opened - CTRL, N
  • Go to Location - CTRL, G
  • Close Book - CTRL, W
  • Search - CTRL, F

Using Assistive Reader

To use these shortcuts, enable Assistive Reader first. Press “Ctrl, A”, or select the Aa menu, then enable Assistive Reader.

  • Toggle Text-to-Speech - Ctrl, T
  • Pause or resume reading - Space bar (while in a book)
  • Read the previous sentence - Ctrl, Shift, Up arrow
  • Skip forward one sentence - Ctrl, Shift, Down arrow
  • Increase speech rate - Shift, +
  • Decrease speech rate - Shift, -
  • Toggle continuous reading - Ctrl, Shift, C

Note: This feature only works on eligible Kindle content. You can configure the Assistive Reader language from the System Preferences on your computer.

Remove Books from Your Mobile Kindle App

Remove or delete a book to clear space on your home screen library.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select and hold the book cover.
  3. Select the Menu icon (three dots), and then Remove Download.
    • To return a Kindle Unlimited book, select Return to Kindle Unlimited. Returning a book removes it from your library.
    • To return a Prime Reading book, select Return. Returning a book removes it from your library. If you wish to permanently keep a copy of the book, you can purchase it.

Remove Items from Collections in Kindle for Windows

How to remove books or documents from a Collection.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  1. Open the Kindle for Windows app.
  2. Select one or more items.
  3. Select Remove from Collection, then select the name of the collection.
  4. Select Remove.

Sync Local Collections in the New Kindle for Mac App

Trying to sync local collections in the new Kindle for Mac App? Follow these steps:

  1. Open the local collection that you want to sync.
  2. Select Sync to account on the banner at the top of the collection window.

Sync your Kindle for Mac App

Sync your app to keep your library up to date.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select More from the navigation bar.
  3. Select Sync Now. The last date synced updates to today’s date and time, confirming the sync is complete.

Sync Your Kindle for Windows App

Sync your app to keep your library up to date.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

  1. Make sure that your computer is connected to the internet.
  2. Open the Kindle for Windows app.
  3. In the left navigation bar, select Library.
  4. Select the sync icon .
    1. The icon stops spinning once the sync completes

Tip:

To sync from anywhere in the Kindle for Windows app, use the F5 keyboard shortcut.

For more keyboard shortcuts, select the Settings icon from the top menu toolbar, then select Keyboard shortcuts. Alternatively, go to Kindle for Windows Keyboard Shortcuts.

Sync Your Mobile Kindle App

Sync your app to keep your library up to date.

Tip: Make sure that your mobile device is connected to the internet.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select More from the navigation bar.
  3. Select Sync now.

The last date synced updates to today’s date and time, confirming the sync is complete.

Turn On Sync for Your Android Kindle App

Easily pick up where you left off across your apps and devices. Access your notes and highlights regardless of which device or app you use.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select More from the navigation bar.
  3. Select Settings (gear icon on top right).
  4. Select the checkbox next to Sync for Books.

A check mark confirms that you turned on Sync.

For more on how Amazon handles your information, go to our Privacy Notice.

Turn On Sync for Your iOS Kindle App

Easily pick up where you left off across your Kindle apps and devices. Access your notes and highlights regardless of which device or app you use.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select More from the navigation bar.
  3. Select Settings (gear icon on top right).
  4. Switch Sync to the on position.

The background turns green when Sync is on.

For more on how Amazon handles your information, go to our Privacy Notice.

Turn On Sync for Your Kindle for Mac App

Easily pick up where you left off on your Kindle apps and devices. Access your notes and highlights regardless of which device or app you use.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select More from the navigation bar.
  3. Select Settings.
  4. Switch Sync to the on position. The background turns blue when Sync is on.

For more on how Amazon handles your information, go to our Privacy Notice.

Use TalkBack with the Kindle App on Android

Kindle Reading app on Android supports the TalkBack accessibility feature.

After enabling TalkBack, you can explore the Kindle Reading app with audio prompts from TalkBack. Audio support is also provided for books and other features.

Note: TalkBack is a feature developed by Google for the Android operating system. For more information about accessibility features on Android devices, visit Google’s TalkBack help pages at 0.

  1. Go to Settings on your Android device.
  2. Tap Accessibility, then tap TalkBack.
  3. Turn TalkBack on or off. After you turn on TalkBack, spoken feedback begins immediately.

Table of TalkBack Actions and Gestures:

  • Read continuously and have the pages turn automatically.
    • Gesture: Swipe down then right to open the TalkBack menu. Then, choose “Read from focused item”.
  • Pause or resume reading.
    • Gesture: Tap once with two fingers.
  • Go to the next content element.
    • Gesture: Swipe right using one finger.
    • Notes: Usually, this action refers to the next paragraph, but it can also be an image, a link, a math equation, or something else.
  • Go to the previous content element.
    • Gesture: Swipe left using one finger.
    • Notes: Usually, this action refers to the previous paragraph, but it can also be an image, a link, a math equation, or something else.
  • Go to the next page.
    • Gesture: Swipe left with two fingers.
  • Go to the previous page.
    • Gesture: Swipe right with two fingers.
  • Open the Book Actions menu.
    • Gesture: Double tap with one finger.
    • Notes: This option is only available when a book is open. The Book Actions menu provides access to many things. This includes the close book button, the table of contents, search, notebook, reading settings, and more.
  • Select text.
    • Gesture: Double tap and hold.
    • Notes: To select a specific word, first navigate by words and put focus on the word you want to select.
  • Navigate by characters.
    • Gesture: Swipe up then down, or down then up. Continue until Characters is selected.
  • Navigate by words.
    • Gesture: Swipe up then down, or down then up. Continue until Words is selected.
  • Navigate by lines.
    • Gesture: Swipe up then down, or down then up. Continue until Lines is selected.
  • Navigate by headings.
    • Gesture: Swipe up then down, or down then up. Continue until Headings is selected.
    • Notes: Headings and their levels are announced and navigable when heading tags are included in the eBook.
  • Go to the next item in granular reading.
    • Gesture: Swipe down with one finger.
  • Go to the previous item in granular reading.
    • Gesture: Swipe up with one finger.

Use VoiceOver with the Kindle App on iOS

The Kindle reading app for iOS supports the VoiceOver accessibility feature.

With VoiceOver turned on, audio support is provided for many books and features on your iOS device.

Note: VoiceOver was developed by Apple for the iOS operating system. For more information about using VoiceOver, go to Use VoiceOver Gestures on iPhone on Apple support.

To turn VoiceOver, developed by Apple for the iOS operating system on or off:

  1. From your iOS device Home, select the Settings icon.
  2. Select Accessibility.
  3. Under Vision, select VoiceOver.
  4. Select On or Off. You can also change other general settings for VoiceOver on this screen.

Use the following table to learn more about the actions and gestures available with VoiceOver:

  • Read continuously and have the pages turn automatically.
    • Gesture: Swipe down using two fingers.
  • Pause or resume reading.
    • Gesture: Tap once with two fingers.
  • Go to the next content element.
    • Gesture: Swipe right using one finger.
    • Notes: Usually, this action refers to the next paragraph, but it can also be an image, a link, a math equation, or something else.
  • Go to the previous content element.
    • Gesture: Swipe left using one finger.
    • Notes: Usually, this action refers to the previous paragraph, but it can also be an image, a link, a math equation, or something else.
  • Go to the next page.
    • Gesture: Swipe left with three fingers.
  • Go to the previous page.
    • Gesture: Swipe right with three fingers.
  • Open the Book Actions menu.
    • Gesture: Double tap with one finger.
    • Notes: This option is only available when a book is open. The Book Actions menu provides access to many things. This includes the close book button, the table of contents, search, notebook, reading settings, and more.
  • Select text.
    • Gesture: Double tap and hold.
  • Navigate by characters.
    • Gesture: Twist two fingers to access the rotor and then select Characters .
  • Navigate by words.
    • Gesture: Twist two fingers to access the rotor and select Words .
  • Navigate by lines.
    • Gesture: Twist two fingers to access the rotor and select Lines .
  • Navigate by headings.
    • Gesture: Twist two fingers to access the rotor and select Headings .
    • Notes: Headings and their levels are announced and navigable when heading tags are included in the eBook.
  • Go to the next item in granular reading.
    • Gesture: Swipe down with one finger.
  • Go to the previous item in granular reading.
    • Gesture: Swipe up with one finger.
  • Navigate math content.
    • Gesture: Twist two fingers to access the rotor and select Symbols , Small Expressions , Medium Expressions , or Large Expressions .
  • Use VoiceOver Gestures on iPhone
  • Enable Reading Ruler on Your iOS Kindle App

Parental controls and child profiles

Create a Child Profile on Your Kindle E-Reader

Set up a child profile for Amazon Kids to protect your child from inappropriate content.

If your compatible Kindle is in Amazon Kids, go to the menu, select Exit Amazon Kids, and enter your parental controls pin to add another child profile.

  1. From the Home screen, select the Menu icon.
  2. Select Parental Controls, then select Amazon Kids.
  3. Select Add New Profile or Add Kid Profile.
  4. Enter your parental controls pin code, if needed, then the child’s name and birthday.
  5. Select Next and add any content from your own library that you want to include in the child’s library.
  6. Review the child’s profile and reading settings, then select Done.

Note: Kindle Scribe doesn’t support Child Profiles at this time, but you can set a parental control PIN to manually lock the web browser, Kindle and Audible stores, Cloud content and/or Goodreads on Kindle.

To create a parental control PIN:

  1. From the home screen, swipe down to open the Quick Actions or select Menu.
  2. Select Settings or All Settings.
  3. Go to Your/My account and select Parental control.
  4. Select Parental control PIN and follow the steps on the screen to enter and save a parental control PIN.

Edit a Child Profile on Your Kindle E-Reader

Want to change what content your child has access to? Update your children’s profile for Amazon Kids on your compatible Kindle.

Manage your children’s profile using the online Parent Dashboard at:

Parent Dashboard

  1. From the Home screen, select the Menu icon.
  2. Select Parental control, then select Amazon Kids.
  3. Select the drop-down menu or the edit option next to the child profile. You have options to change various aspects of your child’s profile, including age, educational level, and share books from your library.

Note: Kindle Scribe doesn’t support Child Profiles at this time.

How to Set Up Your Kindle 11th Generation Kids Edition

Turn on your Kindle E-Reader and follow the on-screen instructions to set it up.

Table of Contents

  • Basic Requirements
  • How to Set Up Your Device
  • Kindle Controls
  • Troubleshoot Setup Issues
  • Accessibility Features on Kindle E-Reader
  • You’re Set Up, What’s Next?
  • Video Guide to Set Up Your Device
  • Safety and Compliance Information for Kindle 11th Generation Kids Edition
  • Warranty Information

Basic Requirements

To start, have the following parts, accessories, and information ready:

  • The Kindle 11th Generation Kids Edition and in-box accessories
  • An Amazon account, including your email address or phone number and password
    • Don’t have an account? You can make one at: Create an Amazon Account
  • An internet connection, including your Wi-Fi name and password

How to Set Up Your Device

Video guide to set up your device

To set up your device:

  1. Select your device language.
  2. Select your location.
  3. Select either Set up on this Kindle, OR Set up with your phone.
  4. Connect to Wi-Fi.
  5. Follow the on-screen instructions to register your Kindle to your Amazon account.
  6. Activate your Amazon Kids+ Subscription
    • For more information, visit our page What Is Amazon Kids.
  7. Create child profiles and set a parental control PIN
    • For more information, visit our page Create a Child Profile on Your Kindle E-Reader.

Set up with your phone: Use Simple Setup with the Kindle app on your iOS or Android device to register your Kindle. To do this, you need the following:

  1. Your Kindle 11th Generation Kids Edition.
  2. The Kindle app running on your iOS or Android device.
  3. Amazon Device Simple Setup turned ON in the Kindle app.
  4. Bluetooth turned ON on your phone, and Location Services turned ON (Android only).

Your Kindle app automatically detects your Amazon account and any previously saved Wi-Fi passwords to help with setup.

Kindle Controls

Use your Kindle 11th Generation Kindle Kids Edition with only a few controls.

Screenshot: Illustration describing the power button and USB-C port of the KIndle 11th Generation Kids Edition

  • 1. USB-C/power port
    • You can use the supplied USB cable to connect your Kindle to a computer for charging the battery. You can also use the USB cable to transfer files to your Kindle. Learn more at our page Connect, Browse, and Transfer Files on E-Reader
  • 2. Power Button
    • To turn your Kindle on, press the Power button located on the bottom edge. To charge your Kindle from a power outlet, you will need the Amazon 5W charger (sold separately) for the USB cable. To turn off your Kindle screen, press and hold the Power button for 9 seconds until the Power dialog box displays, then select Screen Off .
  • 3. Kid-friendly cover
    • The cover opens and closes to wake the Kindle Kids Edition or put it to sleep, and it is available in a range of kid-friendly designs.

Troubleshoot Setup Issues

If setting up your device doesn’t go as expected, here are a few suggestions:

  • If you get a login error or have forgotten your Amazon account password, you can reset your password. Go to our page Forgot Your Password.
  • If you have problems registering your Kindle to your Amazon account, try the troubleshooting steps available at this page: Get Help to Register Your Kindle E-Reader.
  • If you can’t find your network or connect to Wi-Fi, you can find more troubleshooting available on our page Resolve Wi-Fi Connection Issues on Your Kindle E-Reader .
  • If you’re having issues with your Wi-Fi password, contact your internet service provider.

Resetting your device removes all your personal information including lock screen passcode, parental controls settings, and downloaded content.

Note: Determine what Kindle E-reader model you’re using before resetting. Refer to our page Identify Your Kindle E-Reader.

  2. Press the Enter key (below the Del key).

Forgot your Parental Controls password? Reset it from your device using the following steps.

Resetting your device removes all your personal information including lock screen passcode, parental controls settings, and downloaded content.

Note: Determine which Kindle e-reader model you’re using before resetting. Refer to our page Identify Your Kindle E-Reader.

  1. Tap the passcode field.
  2. Type 111222777, and then tap OK.

Setting up and using a Kindle e-reader

2G and 3G E-Reader Network Support FAQ

Starting in 2021, some prior generation Kindle E-Readers will not be able to connect to the internet using cellular connection through 2G or 3G networks.

What is happening? / Why is my cellular connectivity not working?

Mobile Network Operators (MNOs) are phasing out their 2G and 3G networks. Kindle devices that require cellular connectivity through 2G or 3G networks for internet connectivity will be unable to connect to the internet after these networks are discontinued.

How does this impact my Kindle device?

The following devices will no longer be able to connect to the internet:

  • Kindle (1st and 2nd Generation)
  • Kindle DX (2nd Generation)

Because these devices do not have Wi-Fi capability, they will be unable to connect to the internet after 2G and 3G networks are discontinued. You will still be able to enjoy previously downloaded content without internet connectivity.

The following devices will still be able to connect to the internet using Wi-Fi:

  • Kindle Keyboard (3rd Generation)
  • Kindle Touch (4th Generation)
  • Kindle Paperwhite (5th Generation)/(6th Generation)/(7th Generation)
  • Kindle Voyage (7th Generation)
  • Kindle Oasis (8th Generation)

This connectivity change does not impact any Kindle device other than those listed above.

If you are unsure what device you have, visit Identify Your Kindle E-Reader for more information.

Can I upgrade my device?

Learn more about our trade-in program here: Amazon Trade In Program.

Browse and compare Kindle E-readers here: Amazon Trade In Program.

Can I recycle my device?

If you want to recycle your device, refer to Recycling Your Amazon Device. Through this program, Amazon covers all the costs associated with shipping and recycling your Amazon devices and accessories.

Adjust the Screen Light on Your Kindle E-Reader

Some Kindle devices allow you to change how bright or dim your screen appears.

To determine what Kindle e-reader model you’re using, refer to our page Identify Your Kindle E-Reader.

  1. From the home screen, swipe down to open the Quick Actions menu or select the Settings icon. If reading a book, tap the top of the screen.
  2. To adjust the brightness, use the scale or the + or - buttons for gradual change. To maximize or turn off the front light, long press of the + or - buttons on compatible Kindle devices. Alternatively, use the Brightness and warmth option in the Screen and brightness setting menu to configure brightness and warmth. Compatible Kindle devices have a setting above the screen light controls to activate Dark Mode.

Ask Notebooks on Kindle Scribe

Search through your notebooks, obtain AI-powered insights, and refine your search with the Ask Notebooks GenAI feature.

Note: To use this feature on your Kindle Scribe:

  • You need a Kindle Scribe released in 2025 or later.
  • You need to register your Kindle Scribe to an Amazon account and connect it to the internet.

The universal search displays results from the Kindle Store, your Library, and AI insights from your notebooks.

Trigger the Ask Notebooks GenAI feature from the AI-powered insights results category and refine your search. Type in or handwrite questions on the canvas that opens at the bottom of the screen and ask follow-up questions.

Search answers use only information from your notebooks. They’re a quick way to search through your own notes.

Use the Add to notebook option to paste the response generated to your notebooks. Customize the font family, font size and line spacing, and choose to add the response to the first or the last page of your notebook.

Use the New chat option at the top to end the current chat conversation and start a new one.

You can find your previous chat conversations in the Chat history tab at the top. Kindle Scribe saves chat history for only 30 days. To save the answers permanently, add them to your notebooks.

Connect Kindle Scribe to Google Drive, Microsoft OneDrive, or Microsoft OneNote

Import and annotate compatible files between Google Drive and Microsoft OneDrive or upload your notebooks as text or PNG images to Microsoft OneNote.

Note: To use these services on your Kindle Scribe:

  • You need a Kindle Scribe released in 2025 or later.
  • You need to register your Kindle Scribe to an Amazon account and connect it to the internet.
  • You need a Google or Microsoft account.

Connect Kindle Scribe to Google Drive, Microsoft OneDrive, or Microsoft OneNote:

  1. On your Kindle Scribe, tap the connections icon in the main toolbar, then tap Add connections. Alternatively, tap All Settings, and then Connections.
  2. Tap Connect next to the service you want to connect. Two options show up: - Scan a QR code and sign in to Amazon. Use the phone number or email account registered with the Kindle Scribe. - Or visit Kindle Connections (https://www.amazon.com/kindle/connections) on any device and enter the code shown on your Kindle Scribe.
  3. To complete the connection, follow the steps on the screen.

The verification code expires after 15 minutes. You can generate a new code on your device if needed.

To Manage Your Kindle Scribe Connections On-Device:

  1. To access your connected cloud drives, tap the connections icon in the main toolbar.
  2. Tap Manage connections to view the service connection settings. Tap the connection that you want to manage.

You can:

  • Edit the Display name for the connection.
  • View which Microsoft or Google account you’re using by the masked email address displayed.
  • View details of the initial connection time and date.
  • Set default settings for the layout of imported Word files.
  • Choose whether to add the files to both Library and Workspace. By default, we add imported files to the Library only.
  • Disconnect to remove the third-party service connection from your Amazon account.

To disconnect the connections to third-party apps off-device, visit: Kindle Connections on any device.

For more information, read through the services Frequently Asked Qestions (FAQs) at: Kindle Connections

Connect Your Kindle E-Reader to Internet

Make sure you’re within range of the network you want to connect to.

  1. From the home screen, swipe down to open the Quick Actions or select the Menu icon.
  2. Select All Settings or Settings, then Wi-Fi & Bluetooth or Wireless.
  3. Select Wi-Fi Networks and detected Wi-Fi networks appear. If your Wi-Fi network is configured to be hidden or broadcast is deactivated, it won’t be displayed in the list.
  4. Tap on desired Wi-Fi network or select Other to add it manually (for a hidden network).
  5. Enter the name of your network if applicable and then your Wi-Fi password or network key.
  6. Select Connect.

Note: Public hotspots may require a separate sign-in, usually through a pop-up. Load the service provider welcome page and enter the details to connect. Some public networks require a PIN, voucher code, or a One Time Password (OTP). Check with the service provider for more details.

Note: Select Kindle models support mobile carrier connectivity using 4G LTE. To see if your device supports free cellular mobile connectivity:

  1. Go to Settings.
  2. Select Device Options, and then select Device Info.
  3. Select Network Capability.

Supported devices switch seamlessly between Wi-Fi and cellular connection based on network availability, connection quality, and available cellular carrier support.

Conserve Your Kindle E-Reader Battery

Wondering why your Kindle doesn’t stay charged?

Is your Kindle new or recently set up? In the first few days as you download books and install updates, the battery drains. Please keep your Kindle charging during this time.

After set up, use our tips to conserve battery power:

  • Lower your device’s light settings: Devices with a built-in light use more battery.
  • Turn off your device’s internet connection when not in use.
  • Make sure to use a compatible USB cable to charge your Kindle (preferably the one that came with it).
  • Put your device to sleep when done reading. Press the power button once briefly.
  • Keep your device charging during large downloads.
  • Turn on Power saver in the Device options. The device enters in a low-power sleep mode when it hasn’t been used for a few hours.
  • Keep your device charging for a period of time after large downloads. After downloading, your Kindle indexes your books to provide search capability, but it affects battery life.

Note: Your Kindle automatically updates when it’s not in use, affecting battery life.

  • Adjust the Screen Light on Your Kindle E-Reader
  • Turn Off the Internet on Your Kindle E-Reader

Create a Folder or Notebook on Kindle Scribe

Kindle Scribe allows you to create notebooks for you to jot down your thoughts, take notes, or create To-do lists. Notebooks get auto-saved when you close them.

You can organize your notebooks using folders on the Notebook tab. Your device must have at least 100 MB of available storage at all times to be able to create and save new folders and notebooks or new strokes on notebooks.

Tap the Notebook icon next to the Search Toolbar in the upper right corner of the screen.

Alternatively, in the Notebook tab, tap the + icon in the upper right corner of the screen to create a notebook or create a folder.

If these steps don’t solve your issue, press and hold the Kindle power button to prompt the restart menu and restart the device.

  • Delete a Folder or Notebook on Kindle Scribe

Customize Your Kindle E-Reader Text Display

Adjust the appearance of books and periodicals on Kindle E-Readers 6th Generation and higher.

To access text display settings when reading:

  1. Tap the top of the screen to bring up the toolbars.
  2. Select the Page Display icon Aa.

The menu tabs offer a range of reading personalization settings to customize your book display. Your preferences apply to all the books that you read on your Kindle.

Display Settings may vary across Kindle device models.

Tap the Font tab to:

  • Select from a list of available fonts. The Publisher Font option is available on books that have a font embedded by the publisher.

  • Increase or decrease the font size by dragging your finger along the slider or tapping the plus or minus signs.

    You can also change the font size while reading by using a pinching motion.

  • Make any font bold and adjust between five levels of bold. Set your preference by dragging your finger along the slider or tapping the plus or minus signs.

Tap the Layout tab to:

  • Adjust the spacing between lines, paragraphs, words, and.characters.
  • Increase or decrease the margin size.
  • Select portrait or landscape screen orientation for reading.
  • Change text alignment. Options include justified and left justified.

Tap the More tab to:

  • Select your preferred reading progress tracking option.
  • Select whether to show the clock while reading.

Tap the Themes tab to:

  • Switch the page background color.
  • Select your preferred theme settings.
  • Save your current settings for Font, Layout, and More options.

Your Kindle also applies kerning, refines layout for large fonts, and automatically improves line spacing for faster, easier reading across all fonts.

Delete a Folder or Notebook on Kindle Scribe

Remove download or permanently delete options are available depending on whether your Kindle Scribe is connected to the internet or not.

Kindle Scribe doesn’t support multi-selecting several notebooks for deletion.

  • To delete a notebook or folder, select the three-dot menu. A checkmark on the notebook cover shows that the notebook is downloaded on your device.

    • Permanently delete removes the notebook from all Kindle devices and apps. You can’t recover notebooks after permanently deleting it. The option isn’t available for notebooks or folders created while Sync is off.
    • Remove download removes the notebook from the device and frees up space, but keeps the notebook accessible and shows a default cover of the notebook to easily download again.

Any notebooks or documents you share as attachments with Alexa using Send to Alexa will remain on Alexa and can be reviewed, downloaded, or deleted using Review Attachments at: Alexa Privacy.

  • Fix Issues with Notebooks on Kindle Scribe

If you no longer wish to use your Kindle E-reader, you can remove your Amazon account from the device.

To deregister, use the Deregister button after selecting your device on this page:

Manage Your Devices

Note: Before gifting or selling your device, we recommend a Factory Reset to remove all content and user settings from the device. Visit our page Factory Reset Your Kindle E-Reader for instructions.

  1. From the home screen, swipe down to open the Quick Actions or select Menu.
  2. Select Settings or All Settings.
  3. Go to Your/My Account and select Deregister/Deregister Device.
  4. If prompted, select Deregister again to confirm.

To troubleshoot a problem or access a different library, deregister an Amazon account from your Kindle for Web app.

Double Tap to Turn Pages or Advance Your Screen on Kindle E-Reader

Double tap your Kindle’s sides or back to turn pages and scroll through Home and Library screens.

Visit this page to identify your Kindle device: Identify Your Kindle E-Reader.

Kindle Colorsoft Signature Edition and Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition (12th Generation) - 2024 release allow you to turn pages with one hand, using accelerometer-based tap detection.

When this feature is on, tapping twice in quick succession anywhere on the side or back of the device, advances the page. In a book, this means turning the page forward. If you use the feature while on the Home screen and Library, double tapping moves you to the next section of your Home or Library.

To turn on the double tap feature:

  1. Go to All Settings.
  2. Select Device Options, then switch the toggle next to Double tap to on.

Note: Amazon has tested double tap with the official Amazon Kindle Colorsoft and Kindle Paperwhite cases but it may not work with all third-party cases.

Download Collections to Your Kindle E-Reader

Collections created on your registered device or the website don’t automatically download to your other devices. Change your home screen view to reveal your Collections.

  1. From the home screen of your Kindle, select Library.
  2. Select the Sort By Menu in the upper right-hand corner of your screen, then select Collections.
  3. Select the Menu icon on the Collection that you wish to download then select Download all items.

Factory Reset Your Kindle E-Reader

A factory reset (or factory default) is suggested before gifting or selling a device, and in some cases for troubleshooting.

Important:

A factory reset returns the device to its original factory settings, removing all your personal data, downloaded content, and content that hasn’t been synced.

If you use the VoiceView Over Bluetooth feature, you’ll need to download the file to your device from our page: Download the VoiceView Audio File for Your Kindle (English Only).

  1. From the home screen, swipe down to open the Quick Actions or select Menu.
  2. Select Settings or All Settings.
  3. Go to Device Options or select Menu.
  4. Select Factory Reset. For older devices, select Reset Device again.
  5. When prompted, select Yes to confirm.

Upon completion of a factory reset, your device is deregistered from your Amazon account. To use the device, register it again.

Fix Issues Sharing Notes and Notebooks from Your Kindle Scribe

It isn’t possible to access the content from the emailed link. Content shared is incomplete, doesn’t reflect the latest updates, is the wrong file.

Share links expire 7 days after its generation from Kindle Scribe.

If the link expired, the content is incomplete or doesn’t show the latest updates or notes, share the file again from Kindle Scribe.

Fix Issues with Handwriting Recognition on Kindle Scribe

Handwriting conversion currently recognizes cursive, print, and a few emoticons. Make sure that the handwriting recognition language you have selected matches the language in your Notebook.

  • Confirm that your device is on the latest software version. If your device is on version 5.16.2 or higher and the option isn’t displayed, restart your device.
  • Make sure that your device is connected to the internet.
  • If you receive multiple notifications of a sync and convert error, remove the notebook from the device. Redownload the notebook again by tapping on the notebook cover. After successfully redownloading the notebook, try converting the notes again.
  • The Convert to text and quick send to [your registered email] option requires an email associated with the account that you used to register your Kindle Scribe. If no email is associated, the Convert to text and email option isn’t available.
    • To add an email: Log in to your Amazon account on a web browser. From the Account & Lists drop-down, select Account > Login & Security.
  • Confirm that your notebook and notes are in a supported language. A list of currently supported languages is available at our page Convert Handwritten Notes to Text.
  • A searchable PDF of your notebook doesn’t include any edits made during preview in the Convert to text and email option.

Note: Converting larger synced notebooks (100+ pages) can take over 10 minutes to process.

Note: Currently not supported: writing that’s slanted, writing using the highlighter, writing in landscape mode, drawings, diagrams, and equations.

Fix Issues with Notebooks on Kindle Scribe

Deleted Notebooks still appearing on device, page numbers not displayed, template changed, disappeared, or isn’t getting updated.

  • Make sure you’re connected to the internet on your Kindle Scribe and Kindle app so notebook content reflects the latest edits made on your Kindle Scribe when opening it on your Kindle app or phone. If you still can’t access the latest updates:

    • Try refreshing/reopening the notebook in the app.
    • Try manually syncing your Kindle Scribe by tapping on Sync in the Quick Actions menu. Wait a few seconds for the content to be updated.

  • Refresh the Notebook tab view.

  • Delete the notebook on Kindle Scribe again, then sync.

  • Close and reopen the notebook.

  • If you have another Kindle Scribe device registered to the same Amazon account, check that notebooks and folders haven’t been deleted from that device.

  • If your notebook template changed, disappeared or isn’t showing properly, select the template that you want for your notebook again, then close and reopen the notebook.

  • If the issue with a template persists, remove the notebook from the device and download it again.

  • If the page number isn’t displayed on your notebook, tap on the bottom left-hand corner to turn on the page number display.

  • Kindle Scribe doesn’t support copy/pasting images, bookmarking specific pages, and creating a table of content in notebooks at this time.

  • Create a Folder or Notebook on Kindle Scribe

  • Delete a Folder or Notebook on Kindle Scribe

  • Move a Notebook on Kindle Scribe

Fix Issues with Your Notes on Kindle Scribe

Solve common issues with creating a new sticky note, sticky note or active canvas icon missing from toolbar, note not saved, can’t view note or note updates, note won’t open or is frozen, note gets added at the wrong place, accidentally deleted notes, writing without a pen.

Note: If you have two or more Kindle Scribe devices, make sure to have the latest software version installed on both to be able to see active canvases, sticky notes, and expandable margins.

  • Make sure that you have selected the sticky note tool in the writing toolbar
  • Make sure that there isn’t already another sticky note or active canvas at the same location where you’re trying to create a new one
  • Make sure that you’re not adding your sticky note on top of an image or on the margin
  • Unhide the writing toolbar using the 3-dot menu on the top navigation bar
  • Make sure that the content type that you’re annotating supports adding sticky notes, expandable amrgins, or active canvases
  • Check that your note isn’t blank before closing it. Blank sticky notes don’t get saved.
  • Make sure to select the checkmark after you finish your canvas note in the translucent box
  • Flip the page back and forth and check again if the note has been saved
  • Close and reopen the sticky note
  • Close and reopen the book or document
  • Try inserting a new note at a different place or document
  • Press the power button to put the device to sleep and press it again to reawaken the device
  • If you’re unable to find a note you inserted in the All notes view page, make sure that the note that you’re searching for is present on that specific book or document
  • Close the All notes view page and open it again
  • Sync your device tapping the Sync option in the Quick Actions menu
  • If you used a second Kindle Scribe to insert notes in your content, make sure that the device had internet connection. Then sync both devices by tapping the Sync option in the Quick Actions menu.
  • You can’t recover deleted notes. Recreate the note in the same place where you had your previous note.
  • If you lose your stylus pen, you can still tap to insert sticky notes and type in them on content types that support sticky notes. Kindle Scribe only supports handwriting with a stylus pen at this time.
  • On the Kindle app, you can view any highlights or sticky notes you created in books on your device. You can’t view underlines or Active Canvas notes on the Kindle app.

If these tips don’t solve your issue, press and hold the Kindle power button to prompt the restart menu and restart the device.

  • Kindle Personal Document File Types That Support Notes
  • View Your Notes Added to Books and Personal Documents
  • Share Notes and Notebooks from Your Kindle Scribe

Fix Issues with Your Scribe Stylus Pen

Pen is unresponsive or writing without touching the screen, button on the Premium Pen is unresponsive.

Note: The use of 3P covers may interfere with some of the screen writing areas. If using a third-party tablet case containing magnets, remove the case and then try the following steps.

If your Scribe Stylus Pen is unresponsive

  • Make sure that you’re writing on a content type that supports handwritten strokes, sticky notes, or active canvases
  • Choose another writing tool from the writing toolbar
  • Flip the page forth and back
  • Close and reopen the book or Notebook
  • Close and reopen the sticky note
If your Scribe Stylus Pen is writing without touching the screen or the tip is damaged
  • Replace your stylus pen tip using one of the five replacement tips and the tip replacement tool included with your Kindle Scribe. For a graphic step by step go to Kindle Scribe Pen Replacement Tips.
  • If this doesn’t solve the issue, or if you encounter this issue right out of the box, for additional support.
If your Scribe Premium Pen’s button is unresponsive
  • Make sure that the content type where you’re trying to use it supports the shortcut function assigned to the button of your Premium Pen. The stylus pen toolbar shows the pen functions.
  • Make sure that the stylus tip is touching the screen while pressing the stylus pen button.

If these steps don’t solve your issue, press and hold the Kindle power button to prompt the restart menu and restart the device.

  • Kindle Personal Document File Types That Support Notes
  • Fix Issues with Your Notes on Kindle Scribe

Fix Wireless Charging Issues for Your Compatible Kindle Device

Is your compatible Kindle device not charging with your wireless charging device?

The following Kindle devices are compatible with wireless charging:

  • Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition
  • Kindle Colorsoft Signature Edition

Try these steps to solve the problem:

  • Use a “Made for Amazon” charging dock or other compatible Qi-certified charging device. To confirm your charger is compatible, visit this page 0.

  • Be sure to place your Kindle in the center of the charging device. If you’re using a “Made for Amazon” dock, your Kindle should be in portrait mode.

  • Most charging devices have an LED that lights up when the Kindle is charging. Refer to your product guide for more information.

  • Be sure that your charging dock is connected to power.

  • If your Kindle is in a cover: Make sure the cover flap doesn’t get in the way of the charging device, or try removing the cover.

  • Restart your Kindle. Press and hold the power button for 40 seconds or until the device reboots.

  • Identify Your Kindle E-Reader

Get Help to Register Your Kindle E-Reader

Having problems registering your Kindle to your Amazon account? Incorrect device time, outdated software, or incorrect passwords are often the cause.

Want or need a new password? Use the following link:

Forgot Your Password

  1. Confirm that your device displays the correct time at the top of the screen.
  2. Check that your device has the latest Kindle E-Reader Software Updates: 1. From the home screen, select All Settings. 2. Select Device Options, and then select Software updates Or click on the option Software updates from Advanced Options, if available.
  3. Make sure that your device is connected to a Wi-Fi network.
  4. Make sure you’re using your current Amazon account sign-in information and password.
  5. Restart your Kindle.

Setup with your phone: If you have connectivity issues, make sure that your previously saved Wi-Fi password hasn’t changed since you last saved it to your Amazon account.

Tip: If you have multiple accounts, validate your Amazon login credentials on a different device. Navigate to the Amazon website using a web browser and sign into your account.

  • Resolve Wi-Fi Connection Issues on Your Kindle E-Reader
  • Restart Your Kindle E-Reader
  • Resolve Device or App Registration Issues

How to Set Up Your Kindle 11th Generation

Turn on your Kindle E-Reader and follow the on-screen instructions to set it up.

Table of Contents

  • Basic Requirements
  • How to Set Up Your Device
  • Kindle Controls
  • Troubleshoot Setup Issues
  • Accessibility Features on Kindle E-Reader
  • You’re Set Up, What’s Next?
  • Video Guide to Set Up Your Device
  • Safety and Compliance Information for Kindle 11th Generation
  • Warranty Information

Basic Requirements

To start, have the following parts, accessories, and information ready:

  • The Kindle 11th Generation and in-box accessories
  • An Amazon account, including your email address or phone number and password
    • Don’t have an account? You can make one at: Create an Amazon Account
  • An internet connection, including your Wi-Fi name and password

How to Set Up Your Device

Video guide to set up your device

To set up your device:

  1. Select your device language.
  2. Select your location.
  3. Select either Set up on this Kindle, OR Set up with your phone.
  4. Connect to Wi-Fi.
  5. Follow the on-screen instructions to register your Kindle to your Amazon account.
  6. (Kindle 11th Generation Kids only) Activate your Amazon Kids+ Subscription
    • For more information, visit our page What Is Amazon Kids.
  7. (Kindle 11th Generation Kids only) Create child profiles and set a parental control PIN
    • For more information, visit our page Create a Child Profile on Your Kindle E-Reader.

Set up with your phone: Use Simple Setup with the Kindle app on your iOS or Android device to register your Kindle. To do this, you need the following:

  1. Your Kindle 11th Generation.
  2. The Kindle app running on your iOS or Android device.
  3. Amazon Device Simple Setup turned ON in the Kindle app.
  4. Bluetooth turned ON on your phone, and Location Services turned ON (Android only).

Your Kindle app automatically detects your Amazon account and any previously saved Wi-Fi passwords to help with setup.

Kindle Controls

Use your Kindle 11th Generation with only a few controls.

Screenshot: Illustration describing the power button and USB-C port of the KIndle 11th Generation

  • 1. USB-C/power port
    • You can use the supplied USB cable to connect your Kindle to a computer for charging the battery. You can also use the USB cable to transfer files to your Kindle. Learn more at our page Connect, Browse, and Transfer Files on E-Reader
  • 2. Power button
    • To turn your Kindle on, press the Power button located on the bottom edge. To charge your Kindle from a power outlet, you will need the Amazon 5W charger (sold separately) for the USB cable. To turn off your Kindle screen, press and hold the Power button for 9 seconds until the Power dialog box displays, then select Screen Off .

Troubleshoot Setup Issues

If setting up your device doesn’t go as expected, here are a few suggestions:

  • If you get a login error or have forgotten your Amazon account password, you can reset your password. Go to our page Forgot Your Password.
  • If you have problems registering your Kindle to your Amazon account, try the troubleshooting steps available at this page: Get Help to Register Your Kindle E-Reader.
  • If you can’t find your network or connect to Wi-Fi, you can find more troubleshooting available on our page Resolve Wi-Fi Connection Issues on Your Kindle E-Reader .
  • If you’re having issues with your Wi-Fi password, contact your internet service provider.

Accessibility Features on Kindle E-Reader

The Kindle 11th Generation devices has a screen reader feature designed to enhance the experience for customers with disabilities. You can learn more in these resources:

  • Use VoiceView Over Bluetooth on Kindle
  • Guide to VoiceView Gestures on Your Kindle
  • Download the VoiceView Audio File for Your Kindle
  • Kindle E-Reader User and Quick Start Guides

You’re Set Up, What’s Next?

Once you’ve finished setting up your device, you can explore more features to improve your Kindle E-Reader experience. You can learn more in these resources:

  • Buy Books on Your Kindle E-Reader
  • Sync Your Kindle E-Reader
  • Conserve Your Kindle E-Reader Battery
  • Learn More About Ads On Kindle and Fire Tablet

Use your Kindle Scribe with only a few controls.

    • To turn on your Kindle, press the Power button located on the side edge. To turn off your Kindle screen, press and hold the Power button for 9 seconds until the Power dialog box displays, then select Screen Off .

    • Your Kindle comes with an USB cable that you can use to connect your device to a computer for charging.   • 3. Eraser
    • Flip your stylus pen so the eraser at the top is facing the screen. To activate the eraser, press the pen firmly down, then rub the eraser back and forth on the screen to erase the desired strokes.
  • 4. Shortcut button
    • Go to the device’s settings to change the shortcut.

How to Replace Pen Tips:

  1. Remove the old tip using the included replacement tool.
  2. Insert the replacement tip into the opening until it’s seated.

Replace the tips if they’re worn down or the pen writes without touching the screen.

Identify Your Kindle E-Reader

Review distinguishing features to help determine which device you have.

Find and manage your content or device from Manage Your Devices.

Select devices display the device name in the Settings.

  1. From anywhere, swipe down from the top and select All Settings.
  2. Go to Device Options and select Device Info.

Note: Not all Kindle e-reader generations have been available in all marketplaces.

Kindle Scribe Colorsoft (1st Generation)

  • Color Display
  • Premium Pen for handwritten notes included
  • Touchscreen
  • USB-C charging port
  • Adjustable screen light
  • VoiceView screen reader over Bluetooth
  • 32 GB or 64 GB internal storage, with approx 21.18 GB and 49.94 GB respectively available to the user
