Safety and Compliance Information for Kindle Paperwhite Kids

IMPORTANT PRODUCT INFORMATIONSAFETY INFORMATION

READ ALL SAFETY INFORMATION BEFORE USING THE DEVICE. FAILURE TO FOLLOW THESE SAFETY INSTRUCTIONS COULD RESULT IN FIRE, ELECTRIC SHOCK, OR OTHER INJURY OR DAMAGE.

CAUTION – ELECTRICALLY OPERATED PRODUCT: Not recommended for children under 7 years of age.

As with all electric products, precautions should be observed during handling and use to prevent electric shock.

The device and its included accessories are not toys. Children should be supervised to ensure proper use. Parentsshould also routinely check to confirm that the device shows no signs of damage or breakage and is in safe condition for use by children. The device is not suitable for children under age 7.

ALWAYS INSPECT FOR BREAKAGE AND USE WITH CHILD‐FRIENDLY COVER. If the device is dropped or impacted, parents should remove the child‐friendly cover and inspect the device and cover for breaks, chips or cracks before putting the cover back on the device and permitting a child to resume use.

BATTERY SAFETY

HANDLE WITH CARE. Your device contains a rechargeable lithium‐ion battery and should be replaced only by a qualified service provider. Do not disassemble, remove or attempt to modify or access the battery in your device. Avoid dropping the device or battery. If the device or battery is dropped, especially on a hard surface,and the user suspects damage, do not attempt repair. Contact Amazon Customer Service for assistance.

Keep the device and its power adaptor (sold separately) in a well‐ventilated area and away from heat sources, especially when in use or charging. For more information about batteries, go to 0 Your device uses the USB cable provided and a USB charger or USB port that is compliant with safety standards, such as IEC 62368. Do not charge your device near water or in extremely humid conditions.

CHOKING HAZARD – SMALL PARTS.

Small parts contained in your device and its accessories may present a choking hazard to small children.

Protection Against Water

Your device has been tested to withstand brief accidental immersion in water under certain conditions. However,your device is not intended for underwater use and may experience temporary effects from exposure to water. If your device is immersed in water, follow these instructions:

  • Quickly retrieve your device. If your device has a cover, remove the cover.
  • If your device was immersed in fresh water, unplug all cables and drain your device by letting it stand upright with the USB port facing down.
  • If your device was immersed in other types of water, unplug all cables, drain your device by letting it stand upright with the USB port facing down, rinse your device off using fresh water (such as cold tap water) with the USB port facing the water source, and drain your device again.
  • Let the device dry fully in a well‐ventilated location before plugging the USB cable into your device.
  • Do not attempt to dry your device with an external heat source (such as a microwave oven or hairdryer).
  • Do not insert a foreign object into the USB port (such as a cotton bud or paper towel).

To reduce the risk of damage to your device, follow these instructions:

  • Do not intentionally immerse your device in water or expose it to seawater, salt water, chlorinated water or other liquids (such as beverages).
  • Do not spill any food, oil, lotion or abrasive substances on your device.
  • Do not expose your device to pressurised water, high‐velocity water or extremely humid conditions (such as a steam room).

If your device is dropped or otherwise damaged, the waterproofing of the device may be compromised. For more information concerning care instructions and the waterproofing of your device, please see 0

General Care

Clean the screen with a soft cloth; be careful not to wipe it with anything abrasive. When carrying your Kindle in a bag or briefcase, keep a cover on to avoid scratches.

Don’t expose your Kindle to extreme heat or cold. For example, don’t leave it in the trunk of your car in sub‐zero or high‐heat conditions.

Use of third‐party accessories may impact your Kindle device’s performance. In limited circumstances, use of third‐party accessories may void your Kindle device’s limited warranty. Read all safety instructions for any accessories before using them with your Kindle.

Other Safety Considerations

Engaging in repetitive motions such as pressing keys or playing some games may cause you to experience occasionaldiscomfort in your hands, arms, shoulders, neck or other parts of your body. Discontinue use of your device and consult a physician if you experience headaches, blackouts, seizures, convulsion, eye or muscle twitching, loss of awareness, involuntary movement, disorientation or other discomfort. To reduce risk of these symptoms, avoid prolonged use, hold your device some distance from your eyes, use your device in a well‐lit room, and take frequent breaks.

Using your Kindle while operating a vehicle is not recommended and may be prohibited or restricted in some areas. If you are in an area where using your Kindle while operating a vehicle is allowed, always give full attention to the operation of the vehicle and stop using your Kindle if it becomes a distraction.

USE CAUTION AROUND PACEMAKERS AND SIMILAR MEDICAL DEVICES. Personal medical devices, such as pacemakers, may be sensitive to magnetic and electromagnetic fields. Since the Device and certain accessories contain magnets and emit electromagnetic fields, they should be kept at least 15 cm (approximately 6 inches) from any personal medical device. If any interference is observed, consult with your doctor before resuminguse. Items with magnetically‐stored data, such as credit cards and hard drives, may also be sensitive to magnetic or electromagnetic fields and should not be placed near these products.

Wireless Safety and Compliance

Turn your wireless connection off in areas where wireless use is forbidden or when it may cause interference or danger. Some specific situations are described below. In general, you should not use your Kindle with your wireless connection turned on any place you are not allowed to use a cellular device.

Turn Off Wireless When Flying

To prevent possible interference with aircraft systems, the US Federal Aviation Administration regulations and many other aviation agencies require you to have permission from a crew member to use your device’s wireless service.

Using Your Kindle Around Other Electronic Devices

Kindle generates, uses and can radiate radio frequency (RF) energy and, if not used in accordance with its instructions, may cause interference to radio communications and electronic equipment. External RF signals may affect improperly installed or inadequately shielded electronic operating systems, entertainment systems and personal medical devices. While most modern electronic equipment is shielded from external RF signals,if in doubt, check with the manufacturer. For personal medical devices (such as pacemakers and hearing aids), consult with your physician or the manufacturer to determine if they are adequately shielded from external RF signals.

Steps to Minimise Interference

If your Kindle does cause interference to radio or television reception (which you can determine by turning your Kindle off and on), you can try to correct the interference by one or more of the following measures: reorient or relocate the receiving antenna for the radio or television; increase the separation between the radio or television and your Kindle; connect equipment and receivers to different sockets; or consult the radio or television manufacturer or an experienced radio/TV technician for help.

Watch for Signs

There are some places where RF signals could constitute a hazard, such as healthcare facilities and construction sites. If you are not sure, look around for signs indicating that two‐way radios or cell phones should be turned off.

In addition, turn off and do not charge your device if you are in any area with a potentially explosive atmosphere, assparks in such areas could cause an explosion or fire. Such areas are usually, but not always, clearly identified, and include areas where you would normally be advised to turn off your vehicle engine.

AVOID PROLONGED LISTENING AT HIGH VOLUME. Prolonged listening of the player at high volume can damage the ear of the user. To prevent possible hearing damage, users should not listen at high volume levels for long periods.

FCC COMPLIANCE

This device and its related accessories like the adaptor (the “Products”) comply with part 15 of the FCC Rules. Operation of each Product is subject to the following two conditions: (1) each Product may not cause harmful interference,and (2) each Product must accept any interference received, including interference that may cause undesired operation.

Note: The Products have been tested and found to comply with the limits for a Class B digital device or external switching power supply, pursuant to part 15 of the FCC Rules. These limits are designed to provide reasonable protection against harmful interference in a residential installation. The Products generate, use and can radiate radio frequency energy and, if not installed and used in accordance with the instructions, may cause harmful interference to radio communications. However, there is no guarantee that interference will not occur in a particular installation. If a Product does cause harmful interference to radio or television reception, which can be determined by turning the Product off and on, the user is encouraged to try to correct the interference by one or more of the following measures:

  • Reorient or relocate the receiving antenna.
  • Increase the separation between the equipment and receiver.
  • Connect the equipment to a socket on a circuit different from that to which the receiver is connected.
  • Consult the dealer or an experienced radio/TV technician for help.

{47CFR 15.21}

Pursuant to Section15.21 of the FCC rules, changes or modifications to a Product by the user that are not expressly approved by the party responsible for compliance could void the user’s authority to operate the equipment.

Reducing Exposure to Radiation from Radio Equipment.

To reduce radiation exposure from the device, use it in a location where you have strong reception. You can take steps to decrease children’s exposure to radiation from the device by, among other things, limiting the frequency and duration of their use of the device and reducing use at night. Keep the device away from the belly of pregnant women and the lower abdomen of adolescents

FOR CANADIAN CUSTOMERS

Innovation, Science and Economic Development Canada (ISED) Compliance

This device contains licence-exempt transmitter(s)/receiver(s) that comply with Innovation, Science and Economic Development Canada’s licence-exempt RSS(s). Operation is subject to the following two conditions: (1) This device may not cause interference. (2) This device must accept any interference, including interference that may cause undesired operation of the device.

Information Regarding Exposure to Radio Frequency Energy

This equipment complies with IC RSS-102 RF exposure limits set forth for an uncontrolled environment.

This device for operation in the band 5150-5250 MHz is only for indoor use to reduce the potential for harmful interference to co-channel mobile satellite systems.

CAN ICES (B)/NMB (B)

Search Through Content on Your Kindle Scribe

Quickly locate content with Search on Kindle Scribe.

Note: To use this feature on your Kindle Scribe:

  • You need a Kindle Scribe released in 2025 or later.
  • You need to register your Kindle Scribe to an Amazon account and connect it to the internet.

To find content from the universal search:

  1. Tap inside the top search bar from any screen.
  2. Type in key words or handwrite them in the canvas that opens at the bottom of the screen.
  3. Tap Enter ↵ to trigger the search.

The universal search displays results from the Kindle Store, your Library, and your notebooks.

Kindle Scribe searches all notebooks and books in your Library, even the ones not downloaded locally. When you tap a search result from an non-downloaded item, your device prompts you to download it.

Notebook search supports handwritten text in the following languages: English (UK and U.S.), French (Canada and France), German, Spanish, Italian, Dutch, Portuguese (Brazil), Russian, Chinese (Simplified)–Pinyin.

Search results show in categories for simpler identification, and you can use filters and sort options to narrow down your search. Results from your notebooks show page numbers for easier navigation.

  • Ask Notebooks on Kindle Scribe

Send Books to Your Kindle E-Reader Manually

Send books to devices registered to your account.

To send a book to your Kindle e-reader manually:

  1. Log in to the account you used to purchase your book.
  2. On the Content tab of the Manage Your Content and Devices, select Books from the drop down.
  3. Select Deliver or Remove from Device, and you’ll find a list of all your devices registered to that account.
  4. Choose your preferred device or devices, and then Make Changes.

Note: Send documents to your Kindle library on your Kindle devices and Kindle app using Send to Kindle at no additional cost.

Send to Alexa from Kindle Scribe

Send handwritten notes, notebooks, and documents to Alexa.

Note: To use this feature on your Kindle Scribe:

  • You need a Kindle Scribe or Kindle Scribe Colorsoft released in 2025 or later.
  • If interacting with a compatible device such as Echo, it must be registered to the same account as the Kindle Scribe.
  • You need to have access to Alexa+ either through Prime membership or Alexa+ subscription.

You can interact with your notebooks and documents sent from your Kindle Scribe to Alexa. When sent, they will be ready for you to access through Alexa.com, Alexa app, and compatible devices like Echo (Prime membership or Alexa+ subscription is required for use on these devices). Alexa can summarize, answer questions, and create calendar events, reminders, and tasks from the content turning ideas into actions.

How to send a file to Alexa from Kindle Scribe

  1. Open a notebook or document you want to send.
  2. Select the Share button on the top-right corner of your screen.
  3. Select Send to Alexa.

What file types are supported?

From Kindle Scribe, you can send PDF, Microsoft Word (doc, docx), and Notebooks. The maximum file size supported is 25MB.

How do I manage sent files?

You can view and manage your shared files from Alexa Privacy or Alexa Privacy in the Alexa app. Learn more about Alexa attachments at Alexa and Alexa Device FAQs.

Will my sent files automatically sync with Alexa as I update them on Kindle Scribe?

No, only the files you proactively share are sent to Alexa. You will need to reshare updated versions.

Set the Time on Your Kindle E-Reader

Setting the local time on your Kindle e-reader can prevent issues during the device registration.

  1. Connect your device to a Wi-Fi network.
  2. From the home screen, swipe down to open the Quick Actions and select All Settings.
  3. Select Device Options, then select Date and time.
  4. Select time.
  5. Enter the current time and tap OK.
  6. Check the device time at the top of the screen to make sure it displays your local time.

Note: If the time didn’t update properly, restart your device and set up the time again.

Share Kindle Scribe Notes and Notebooks to Google Drive, Microsoft OneDrive, or Microsoft OneNote

Upload a PDF copy of your annotated files to Google Drive or Microsoft OneDrive, or upload your notebooks as text or PNG images to Microsoft OneNote.

Note: To use these services on your Kindle Scribe:

  • You need a Kindle Scribe released in 2025 or later.

  • You need to register your Kindle Scribe to an Amazon account and connect it to the internet.

  • You need a Google or Microsoft account.

    To connect the services visit: Connect Your Kindle Scribe with Google Drive, Microsoft OneDrive, or Microsoft OneNote.

When you share notebooks or documents with Google Drive or Microsoft OneDrive, Kindle Scribe creates a folder to store your shared files.

Share Documents to Connected Drives:

  1. Open the document and tap to display the top toolbox.
  2. Tap the share icon on the top-right corner.
  3. On the share menu at the bottom, choose the drive service you wish to use.
  4. Tap Upload on the next screen that opens.

Kindle Scribe doesn’t support sharing personal documents with Microsoft OneNote.

Share Notebooks to Connected Services:

  1. Open the notebook and tap to display the top toolbar.
    • Tap the share icon on the top-right corner and tap Share current page or Share entire notebook.
    • Alternatively, select the multipage icon in the top toolbar to select the pages you want to share. Then tap the share icon .
  3. On the share menu at the bottom, choose the service you wish to use.
  4. Choose the Format for your shared file and preview the conversion.
  5. Tap Upload at the bottom.

When uploading notebooks to Google Drive or Microsoft OneDrive, choose to keep handwriting as PDF, convert to text, or create searchable PDFs.

When uploading notebooks to Microsoft OneNote, format options include keeping handwriting as a PNG image or converting to text. Choosing both shows them side by side.

Note: Microsoft OneNote saves your shared notebooks to Microsoft OneDrive.

To find your shared notebooks, navigate to My Files > Documents > Kindle Scribe.

For M365 Business accounts, navigate to My Files > Notebooks > Kindle Scribe.

Access to your workplace cloud drive depends on your company’s security settings. Check with your IT team to see if you can connect to your company account.

To learn about other methods to share your documents, visit: Share Notes and Notebooks from Your Kindle Scribe.

For more information, read through the services Frequently Asked Qestions (FAQs) at: Kindle Connections

Share Notes and Notebooks from Your Kindle Scribe

Share your annotations on books and personal documents, and your notebooks by email to coworkers, or other personal contacts. The generated email contains a link that recipients can use to download the exported content within 7 days.

When you share content from your Kindle Scribe, your handwritten or text sticky notes show at the end of the shared document. On-page mark-ups and highlights remain in the original doc.

Kindle Scribe doesn’t support sharing multiple notebooks at once.

    • Open the book, document, or Notebook and tap to display the top bar menu.
    • If in a book, tap the notebook icon.
  2. Tap the Share icon on the top-right corner.
  3. On the Share bottom menu, choose Quick send to [your registered email address] or Share via email. The share link expires after 7 days and the content is no longer available from that link.

Note: Currently, it isn’t possible to share notebooks and personal documents with members of your Amazon Family even if they have a Kindle Scribe too.

When using the Convert to text Share options, a PDF of your notebook is available. You can search for keywords in your handwritten notes when reading the PDF on your phone or laptop.

  • When using the Convert to text and quick send [to your registered email] option, a searchable PDF is available as a second link.
  • When using the Convert to text and email option, select the checkbox Attach notebook as a searchable PDF on the Share via email page.

Note: The searchable PDF of your notebook doesn’t include any edits made during preview in the Convert to text and email option.

Only for Kindle Scribe models released in 2025 or later. For more help, go to:

  • Connect Your Kindle Scribe with Google Drive, Microsoft OneDrive, or Microsoft OneNote
  • Share Kindle Scribe Notes and Notebooks to Google Drive, Microsoft OneDrive, or Microsoft OneNote

Summarize Your Notebooks or Refine Your Writing on Kindle Scribe

Use GenAI to summarize or refine your handwritten notes.

Note: The Summarize your notebooks and Refine your writing features are available on Kindle Scribe only. To summarize or refine content, you need to connect your device to the Internet.

These features are currently available to users in the United States, the United Kingdom, Ireland, Germany, Canada, and Australia.

The summarization and refine writing features work on content written in English, Candian French, and German.

To summarize your notes or refine your writing:

  1. Open a notebook.
  2. Select the GenAI icon in the top toolbar, then select Summarize or Refine writing.
  3. If you select Summarize, select Summarize current page or Summarize the entire notebook, then select Start. If you select Refine writing, select Refine current page or Refine the entire notebook, then select Start.

If your page or notebook has 25 or less words, you get a message that says that GenAI can’t process the summarization.

You can summarize up to 15 pages and refine up to 10 pages of handwritten notes.

If your notebook contains more pages than GenAI can process at once, an error message shows when selecting Summarize or Refine the entire notebook.

You can customize your summary by choosing its legth, tone, and font.

To choose specific pages to summarize or to refine writing:

  1. Select the multipage icon in the top toolbar and long-press on a page to open the multi-select view. While in multi-select view, all the pages that you select show a check mark in their top-left corner.
  2. Select the AI Tools option in the top ribbon, then select Summarize or Refine writing.
  3. If you select Summarize, select Summarize selected pages or Summarize the entire notebook, then select Start. If you select Refine writing, select Refine selected pages or Refine the entire notebook, then select Start.

You can select Cancel instead of Start to go back and start again.

While GenAI is processing the notes, a loading screen appears for a few seconds. Larger notebooks or newly created notebooks may take longer to process as they may need to sync to the cloud.

You get a preview of the page (or pages) generated once your summary or your refined notes are ready. If the generated content runs over more than one page, use the slider on the right side of the page to scroll down.

Select Customize to adjust the font, the font size, and the line spacing.

Select Add to Notebook to insert your summary or refined pages as the first pages or as the last pages in the notebook.

To discard the summarization or refined pages before adding them to the notebook, select the X at the top right of the page. Then confirm that you want to cancel the generation.

Summarization and refined pages have the same properties as regular handwritten notes. You can delete, use the lasso select and all the options in the writing toolbar, and edit them manually.

The feature doesn’t support some tables or diagrams and can’t summarize or refine them.

Sync Your Kindle E-Reader

Seamlessly read across all your devices using sync to download pending books, notes and highlights, software updates, and back up your furthest page read.

First, make sure that your Kindle is connected to the internet.

  1. From the home screen, swipe down to open the Quick Actions or select Menu.
  2. Select the Sync icon or the Sync and Check for Items option.

You can access your highlights and text-based annotations across all Kindle surfaces after you sync. Currently, you can only access your handwritten annotations across Kindle Scribe devices.

Kindle Scribe notebooks are backed up to the Cloud when the device is connected to the internet and Sync is turned on.

If Sync is off, you can’t download notebooks, or backup and save notebooks to the Cloud. To prevent data loss, you’re also unable to permanently delete notebooks in the Cloud using your device.

Turn Off the Internet on Your Kindle E-Reader

Conserve your battery with airplane mode on Kindle (6th Generation and newer) when your device doesn’t need an internet connection.

  1. From the home screen, swipe down to open the Quick Actions menu or select the Settings icon.
  2. Select the Airplane Mode icon or option.

When your Kindle is in Airplane Mode, you can’t buy, sync, or download Kindle content on your device.

Update Your Kindle Book Version

Receive the most updated version available for your Kindle books including corrections and improvements.

  1. Go to Manage Your Content and Devices.
  2. Search for your Kindle book.
  3. If available, select Update Available, then select Update.

Tip: To receive automatic book updates as they become available from the publisher or author, enable Automatic Book Update on the Preferences tab in Manage Your Content and Devices.

Update Your Sync Settings for Kindle

Easily pick up where you left off across your Kindle apps and devices. Access your notes and highlights regardless of which device you’re using.

Important: The Legacy Kindle app (previously called Kindle for PC) isn’t supported as of June 30, 2026. To download the new Kindle for Windows app, go to Install or Update the Kindle App for Desktop.

Sync automatically backs up and saves data from your Kindle devices and apps, including your reading position, notes, and highlights, to Amazon’s servers. Saved data syncs with all Kindle apps and devices linked to your account so you always have it readily available.

Sync, is already enabled by default on all Kindle devices and apps. Your device needs to be connected to the internet for Sync to work.

If you would like to turn Sync off or on, use these steps.

To turn Sync off or on in Kindle apps for iPhone, Android phone, Mac, or Windows :

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select More.
  3. Select Settings (gear icon on top right).
  4. Select Sync, and confirm whether it’s off or on.

To turn Sync off or on in Kindle apps for iPad or Android tablet:

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select the More from the sidebar navigation.
  3. Select Settings.
  4. Select Sync, and confirm whether it’s off or on.

To turn Sync off or on for Kindle E-Readers:

  1. Select More.
  2. Select Settings.
  3. Select Device Options.
  4. Turn Sync off or on, and then confirm your choice.

You can also turn Sync off or on for all Kindle apps and devices linked to your account.

Note: This doesn’t impact the Kindle Scribe. To turn off Sync for Kindle Scribe, refer to the “Turn Sync on or off for Kindle E-readers” section.

  1. Go to Manage Your Content and Devices.
  2. Select the Preferences tab.
  3. Select Sync Settings.
  4. You can now turn this setting off or on.

If you turn off this setting, usage data won’t automatically save to Amazon’s servers. Some features are unavailable, including sharing your reading position across devices, and Export/Share for notes. Also, any notes, highlights, or bookmarks made while Sync is turned off, may be lost when turning this setting back on.

Note: If you use Kindle for Web (read.amazon.com) after turning off Sync, all data generated while you use Kindle Web Reader will continue to save to Amazon’s servers but won’t sync to your other Kindle devices and apps.

For more on how Amazon handles your information, go to our Privacy Notice.

Use a Kindle Audio Adapter with Kindle Paperwhite (7th Generation)

With VoiceView, blind and visually impaired customers can navigate the device using a screen reader and listen to Kindle books compatible with text to speech using the Kindle Audio Adapter.

To determine which Kindle e-reader model you’re using, refer to our page Identify Your Kindle E-Reader.

Note: VoiceView over Bluetooth is available in the following languages: English, French, German, Italian, Portuguese, and Spanish.

If you purchased your Kindle Paperwhite (7th Generation) in a bundle with a Kindle Audio Adapter, connect your adapter and a pair of headphones to your Kindle Paperwhite (7th Generation) to get started. Any Kindle books on your Amazon account that are compatible with VoiceView (text to speech) are available for you to choose from.

Note: VoiceView does not support music or audiobook files.

Your Kindle Paperwhite (7th Generation) should be on software version 5.7.4.1 or higher for VoiceView to work. To download the latest software update for your device, go to our page Kindle Software Updates.

Use Quick Notes on Kindle Scribe

Access Quick Notes from Home and jot down your ideas, thoughts, daily drivers, and sticky reminders.

Note: To use this feature on your Kindle Scribe:

  • You need a Kindle Scribe released in 2025 or later.
  • You need to register your Kindle Scribe to an Amazon account and connect it to the internet.

Quick notes are a type of notebook pinned to your Kindle Scribe Home. It displays the last page used in the notebook.

You can resize the Quick notes on Home by tapping the double chevrons “>>” or “<<.”

Tapping on the plus icon next to or under Quick notes adds a new page to the end of the Quick notes notebook.

Within Quick notes, you can move or copy pages to another notebook by using Move in the top toolbar. You can also delete pages.

You can find the Quick notes notebook in Workspace.

  • Manage Your Kindle Scribe Content and Documents Using Workspace

Use Your Kindle E-Reader in Dark Mode

Make text more readable against a different background mode on your compatible Kindle E-Reader.

You can invert the text color on your Kindle to dark mode. The chosen appearance inverts the text, image, and page color on all screens for Black and White devices, like Kindle, Kindle Paperwhite, and Kindle Scribe.

To switch your Kindle background appearance:

  1. Go to Settings.
  2. Select Screen and brightness, and then select Appearance or Color Style.
  3. Select your preferred mode.

Alternatively, slide down from the top of the screen to bring up Quick Actions and select Dark Mode.

Note: If your PDF doesn’t render properly and hard to read in Dark Mode or with Page Color, turn off the feature to render the PDF in its original format.

Write in the Expandable Margin of eBooks on Kindle Scribe

Open the expandable margin and write directly in it, or create notes anchored to specific passages and view them in the margin of your book.

Note: Expandable margin is currently available on Kindle Scribe only, and supports left-to-right horizontal languages only. This feature doesn’t support right-to-left horizontal languages, such as Arabic, or vertical languages like Japanese currently.

Opening the Expandable Margin

  1. Find the expandable margin icon at the top of either the left or right margin.
  2. Tap the icon to open the expandable margin. It opens on the opposite side of the writing toolbar.

Creating Notes

There are three easy ways to add notes:

  1. Write directly in the Expandable Margin. Your notes automatically anchor to nearby text.
  2. Use the Sticky Note tool from the writing toolbar and tap where you want the note.
  3. Tap any highlight or underline and select “Note” from the menu. Your note gets linked to that specific passage.

Customizing Your Expandable Margin View

You can:

  • Adjust the panel width.
  • Choose between docked view (beside the text) or overlay view (on top of the text).
  • Close the panel completely and have your notes appear as sticky notes on the page. Your sticky notes appear in the expandable margin when it’s open.

If your expandable margin appears to be full, you can always create new notes by using the Sticky Note tool to anchor to text. Or, you can also tap any highlight or underline and select “Note”. The expandable margin automatically creates space for new notes.

  • Use Your Pen to Handwrite on Books and Personal Documents
  • Delete Notes Added to Books and Personal Documents

Troubleshooting

AutoRip Songs Not Showing in Library

We add the MP3 files to Library within 48 hours from when the physical album ships. If you still haven’t received your AutoRip content, check the following:

Note: AutoRip eligible preordered albums’ music is added to Library on the album’s release date.

  1. Locate and review the order for the physical album in Your Account.
  2. Confirm that the item is shipped and sold by Amazon.
  3. Confirm none of the items within the order are marked as a gift.
  4. Select the item to confirm that the AutoRip logo is displayed for that album.
  5. Confirm that your country of residence matches the marketplace you ordered the album from.

Kindle / Reading Apps / Setup / Troubleshooting / Mobile

I’m having trouble setting up my Kindle app on my device. {Keywords: android, iOS, iPad, iPhone, install, installing, set up} [Kindle / Reading Apps / Setup / Troubleshooting / Mobile]

Overview

The Kindle apps for Android and iOS allow customers to read their Kindle library on their mobile device. To set up the app, they must download it from their preferred app store and follow the on-screen registration instructions.

Diagnostic Tips

  • Always make sure the customer’s Kindle app is up to date by running the automatic software update. To check which version an Android device is running:
    1. Open the Kindle app.
    2. Select More > Info > About.
  • Refer the customer to their device manufacturer or service provider (kindle_reading_apps_content_issues_mobile) in the following cases:
    • Wi-Fi or mobile data service issues unresponsive to the troubleshooting steps in scenario 1.
    • Device or device setup issues.
    • Issues persisting after reinstalling the app.
  • For customers with Android Devices:
    • The Kindle for Android app supports Android OS 10.0 and higher.
    • To download books with the Kindle for Android app, customers must:
      • Update their Android OS version to 10.0 or higher.
      • Install the latest version of the Kindle app.
  • For customers with iOS devices:
    • The iOS Kindle app only supports version 16 and higher.
    • Customers with unsupported iOS versions will not be able to re-download the app if they uninstall it.
    • Customers must update their iOS version to download the Kindle app.

Scenarios

  1. I’m trying to sign in but I get an error saying there’s no internet connection (Android).
  2. I’m trying to sign in but I get an error saying there’s no internet connection (iOS).
  3. My Kindle app is named “[NAME]’s 2nd Android/iOS” even though it is the only device of that type in my account.
  4. I want to move my Kindle app to the SD card but I can’t.
  5. I updated my app and now my books are gone.
  6. I updated my app and now it doesn’t work.
  • 1. I’m trying to sign in but I get an error saying there’s no internet connection (Android).
    • What to Do: Ask the customer to restart their device. Make sure that Wi-Fi or mobile data are enabled. Ask the customer to open the Settings app in their device, then go to Wireless & Networks or Connections . Depending on the customer’s device, these options may be different. Ask the customer to turn on Wi-Fi and mobile data off and check if there’s a difference. If not, ask them to turn mobile data on and Wi-Fi off and check again. If the customer can’t complete their download due to a poor connection, ask them to contact their ISP.
    • What to Say: Phone/Chat “I’d like to try a few simple troubleshooting steps to bring your Android device online again.” “Since the steps we tried didn’t work, the next step is for you to contact your internet service provider. They should be able to troubleshoot your problem.”
  • 2. I’m trying to sign in but I get an error saying there’s no internet connection (iOS).
    • What to Do: Ask the customer to restart their device. Go to Settings > Wi-Fi and make sure Wi-Fi is on. If the customer agrees, reset their networks on their iOS device by going to Settings > General > Reset > Reset Network Settings Note: This will reset Wi-Fi networks and passwords, cellular settings, and VPN and APN settings that the customer may have. If the customer can’t complete their download due to a poor connection, ask them to contact their ISP.
    • What to Say: Phone/Chat “I’d like to try a few simple troubleshooting steps to bring your device online again.” “Since the steps we tried didn’t work, the next step is for you to contact your internet service provider. They should be able to troubleshoot your problem.”
  • 3. Kindle for Android app is named “[NAME]’s 2nd Android/iOS” even though it is the only device of that type on the account.
    • What to Do: Ask the customer to change the name of their device in Manage Your Content and Devices. Go to Manage Your Content and Devices: Manage Your Content and Devices (https://www.amazon.com/mycd) Choose the Devices tab. Locate the app and select Edit next to the app’s name. Enter a new name for the app.
    • What to Say: Phone/Chat “You can change the name of your device in Manage Your Content and Devices. Just click Edit next to the name of your mobile phone or tablet.”
  • 4. I want to move my Kindle app to the SD card but I can’t (Android).
    • What to Do: Explain to the customer that the Kindle app for Android can’t be moved to the SD card.
    • What to Say: Phone/Chat “Kindle for Android cannot be installed on your SD card and can only be installed on your device’s internal storage. Kindle content is also stored in your device’s internal memory”.
  • 5. I updated my Kindle app and now my books are gone.
    • What to Do: First, ask the customer to do a manual sync of their app. If that doesn’t work, have the customer download their books again: Open the Kindle app. Select Library > All . Select a book to download it. To send books to a customer device from AC3: Go to the Digital Library section on the customer’s account. Select Books . Enter book title in the search bar, and select Send to Device Select the device the book should be delivered to.
    • What to Say: Phone/Chat “I’m sorry that you’ve had problems upgrading the Kindle for Android reading app”. “I’d like to try a few simple troubleshooting steps to try to solve the issue.”
  • 6. I updated my app and now it doesn’t work.
    • What to Do: Deregister and reregister the app again. Note: Clearing the app data will delete all Kindle content and settings from the device. Clearing app data isn’t possible in iOS. Customers must delete the app and reinstall it instead. The names of menus and settings may differ slightly across Android devices. For Android: Select More > Sign Out . Sign back in again. For iOS: Select More from the navigation bar. Select Settings . Under Registration is the name of the registered account. Next to the name, select Update . Select Deregister this Kindle . Clear app data. For Android: Open the Settings app. Select Apps . Find the All filter to see all apps. Select the Kindle app. Select Storage . Select Clear data . For iOS devices: Skip this step. Uninstall the app and install it back again. For Android: Open the Settings app from the app drawer. Select Apps . Select See all apps . Find the Kindle app and select it. Select Uninstall . When the app is uninstalled, go to your preferred app store. In the search results, select “Amazon Kindle.” Select Install to download and install. Open the app after it’s installed. Sign in. For iOS: Select and hold the Kindle app icon until the app menu appears. Select Delete app. Download and install the app again from iTunes App Store. File a ticket using the following pathway: Kindle > Kindle Reading Apps > Kindle for Android .
    • What to Say: Phone/Chat “I’m sorry that you’ve had problems upgrading the Kindle for Android reading app”. “I’d like to try a few simple troubleshooting steps to try to solve the issue.”

Kindle Disk Error Troubleshooting

Follow the instructions below to resolve the Kindle device disk error.

Verify your Kindle device model and release.

For devices released prior to 2024, excluding Kindle Scribe (Error Code E3)

  1. For Windows: Repair your drives by connecting your Kindle to a computer via USB cable. Click Yes when you see a message asking if you want to repair the Kindle drive.
  2. For Mac OS: Repair your drives by using Spotlight Search (command + space bar) to find the Disk Utility app. Select your Kindle from the list of devices (it might be called NO NAME) and click First Aid.

If repairing your drive does not resolve the error, perform a Factory Reset to return the device to its original factory settings. This will remove all your personal data, downloaded content, and content that hasn’t been synced.

  • Go to Settings > Device Options.
  • Select Factory Reset. For older devices, select Reset Device.
  • When prompted, select Yes to confirm.

Upon completion of a factory reset, your device is de-registered from your Amazon account. To use the device, register it again.

For Scribe and E-readers released in 2024 or later (Error Code E2)

Perform a Factory Reset to return the device to its original factory settings. This will remove all your personal data, downloaded content, and content that hasn’t been synced.

  • Go to Settings > Device Options.
  • Select Factory Reset. For older devices, select Reset Device.
  • When prompted, select Yes to confirm.

Upon completion of a factory reset, your device is de-registered from your Amazon account. To use the device, register it again.

I can’t download Kindle content. {Keywords: smartphone, android, iOS, iPhone, iPad} [kindle_reading_apps_content_issues_mobile]

Hello,

I’m sorry you’re having trouble downloading content.

To solve the problem, here are a few things you can try:

  • Restart your mobile device and try downloading again.
  • Make sure that Wi-Fi or mobile data are on.
  • Make sure that your device meets the minimum requirements:
    • Mobile Devices:
      • iPhone with OS 16
      • Android phones with OS 10.0 or higher
    • Mobile Browsers:
      • Chrome 87f
      • Firefox 78f
      • Safari 11f
      • Opera 61f
      • UC Browser 10.0f
      • Samsung Browser 12.0f
  • Update your Kindle app. You can do this from your preferred app store.
  • Sync your app and try downloading again.
  • Check that your device has enough storage to download content.
  • Uninstall the app and install it back again.

If you are still unable to download your content, please contact us through phone or chat so we can assist you: .

Troubleshoot Kindle Content Not Appearing in Your Library

Can’t find your Kindle books? Fix download issues and try other troubleshooting steps to deliver Kindle books to your library.

Table of Contents

  • Check Internet Connectivity
  • Sync Your Kindle App or Device
  • Send Content to Your Device from Manage Your Content and Devices
  • Confirm the Status of New Purchases
  • Check the Filters on Your Kindle Library
  • Restart Your Kindle Device

Check Internet Connectivity

A working internet connection is required to purchase, download, or sync books to your library.

  1. Verify Wi-Fi Connection: Open your device settings and verify Wi-Fi is connected.
  2. If no connection is detected, make sure that Wi-Fi is turned On and Airplane mode is Off.

For help with connectivity issues on Kindle devices, go to Resolve Wi-Fi Connection Issues on Your Kindle E-Reader.

Sync Your Kindle App or Device

Once you’re connected to Wi-Fi:

Sync Your App:

  1. Open your Kindle app and select More.
  2. Select Sync or Sync Now.

Sync Your Kindle E-Reader

  • Go to Settings.
  • Select Sync My Kindle or Sync and Check for Items.

Wait for sync to complete and then check for your content.

Send Content to Your Device from Manage Your Content and Devices

  1. Go to Manage Your Content and Devices and select ‘Books’ from the menu options at the top of the page.
  2. Look for your new content in the list and select Deliver or remove from device.
  3. Choose your device from the list and select Make Changes.
  4. Sync your Kindle app or device and look for the content in your library.

Confirm the Status of New Purchases

If you can’t find your book in Manage Your Content and Devices, check the payment status of your Digital Order.

  1. Go to Your Orders, then select Digital Orders.
  2. Locate your order and verify if it’s complete.
  3. If your order is incomplete, you can select Retry Payment Method or Change Payment Method.

Check the Filters on Your Kindle Library

Sometimes your content can seem hidden due to active filters.

  1. Open your Kindle app.
  2. Select Library.
  3. then choose All to display your entire library.
  4. Select the Filter button to remove any active filters and refresh your library.
  5. Search for your title.

Tip: Select the Sort icon and select Recent to bring new items to the top. Wait for the library to refresh and search for your title.

Restart Your Kindle Device

If you’re using a Kindle device, restarting can fix common issues. Keep your device charged during the restart.

Press and hold the power button, then select Restart. Wait for your device to restart. If the issue persists, try the steps in Restart Your Kindle E-Reader.

Tip: Make sure that your Kindle has the most recent software update.

Did you know? You can deliver books directly from your account to your device. Check it out on:

Manage Your Content and Devices

  • Resolve Wi-Fi Connection Issues on Your Kindle E-Reader
  • Update Your Payment Method
  • Sync Your Mobile App
  • Sync Your Kindle E-Reader
  • Restart Your Kindle E-Reader
  • Kindle E-Reader Software Updates

Your library and managing content

Check Out or Return a Public Library Book

Kindle books that you borrow from a public library are available to you for a specific period of time, just like with physical library books.

Eligible library books are available in the United States through a digital service called OverDrive. Confirm whether your library branch participates and offers Kindle books.

To check out public library books:

  1. Get a library card and a PIN from your local library.
  2. Go to the website of your local public library and search for “Kindle books” or “eBooks.”
  3. At checkout, sign in to your Amazon account and select the device to which you want to send the book.
  4. Connect your device to Wi-Fi and download the title from the Archived Items or Cloud. Some titles may require USB transfer from your computer to your supported device.

To return a library book and remove it from your devices:

  1. Go to Manage Your Content and Devices (0).
  2. From Your Content, select the Actions button.
  3. Select Return This Book, and then select Yes in the pop-up window.
  4. You will no longer have access to the book from your device after it is returned.

For information on returning your library books to the library site, visit the following help pages:

  • Libby Help - Returning Kindle Books
  • Overdrive Help - How to return a Kindle Book borrowed from your library

Download Books to Your Fire Tablet

Can’t find your book? Kindle Store purchases are saved to the Cloud. Download them anytime from your Amazon account library.

Device not on hand? Download your book from: Manage Your Content and Devices.

  1. Navigate to the Home page of your tablet.
  2. Select Books or open the Kindle app, then select Library.
  3. To see all your previously purchased items, select All from your tablet’s library.
  4. Select the cover of the item you’d like to retrieve to download it again.

Learn About Two-Factor Authentication for Your Kindle Library

Two-factor authentication is a security step that helps ensure you only receive documents from sources you trust.

Need to update Personal Document Settings? You can find this and many other options by going to: .

Manage Your Content and Devices

When you send a document to your Send to Kindle email address, you receive an email asking you to verify your request within 48 hours. Avoid two-factor authentication by making your Send to Kindle email address sufficiently secure.

  • Ensure your Send to Kindle email address is different than any other email addresses you use.
  • Use a combination of uppercase letters, lowercase letters, and numbers.
  • Avoid including any personally identifiable information.

Related Topics

  • Edit Your Send to Kindle Email Address

Machine-Readable Cataloging (MARC) Records and Custom Library Pricing

Amazon Business is now offering MARC records and custom pricing to public libraries in the United States.

Frequently Asked QuestionsWhat features and benefits are available to public libraries purchasing books on Amazon?

MARC records and special book pricing are now available to U.S. public library customers on Amazon Business. As Amazon continues to build new offerings and products to support our library customers, we welcome feedback or recommendations to improve the library shopping experience on Amazon Business. Provide feedback by completing our survey or emailing booksenterprise@amazon.com.

What books discounts are available to public libraries?

Amazon Business U.S. public library customers have access to library-only prices on eligible print books starting when they are available for preorder until 90 days post-publication. Book detail pages will automatically reflect discounts for logged-in U.S. public library customers. Discounts are subject to change and only apply to physical books sold directly by Amazon in the U.S. Discounts may not apply to certain titles.

How do I access MARC records for my book purchases on Amazon Business?

Amazon Business provides brief MARC records for most physical books sold on Amazon. For these books, MARC records will be available to download immediately after placing the order, at no additional cost. After completing a book purchase on Amazon Business, visit 0 to download available MARC records. Bookmark this page for easy access in the future.

What if there isn’t enough inventory available for a certain title?

Amazon Business offers 2 ways to acquire bulk inventory: (1) the Bulk Buying Tool allows you to consolidate inventory from multiple suppliers, and (2) the Request For Quote option allows you to request a discounted price on single-SKU purchases over $10,000 or 999 units. Learn more about bulk purchasing .

Can I use my personal Amazon.com account to access these features?

No. You must log into your public library’s Amazon Business account to download MARC records and access library-only pricing.

Why am I not seeing lower pricing as a public library?I’m part of a U.S. school library, can I access the new library features?

As a U.S. school library, you will have access to available MARC records. Depending on your Amazon Business account type, you may see library-specific or education-specific pricing on books. Contact us at booksenterprise@amazon.com if you’re not seeing any discounts at all.

Missing Songs in Library

If you don’t see recently purchased or added titles in the Library, try refreshing your music or updating the app.

  1. Open the Amazon Music app menu, and select Settings.
  2. Select Refresh My Music.
  • How Do I Find My Purchased Music in My Amazon Music Mobile App?

Remove Highlights in Kindle for Web

Clear colored highlights from text. Highlights are removed from all your Kindle apps and devices.

  • To delete a highlight, click the highlight, then choose the color with an X.
  • Alternatively, click the Notebook icon, then click the Trash icon beside the highlight.

Remove Inappropriate Content from Your Kindle

Get more control of ads and recommendations on your device. Filter ads that may contain images or content not suitable for all audiences.

  1. From the home screen, swipe down to open the Quick Actions or select Menu.
  2. Select Settings or All Settings.
  3. Select Your Account.
  4. Select Lockscreen ads.
  5. Toggle Filter Ads or Filter Special Offers to On or Off.

Share Content Using Family Library

Amazon Family members can access more eBooks and other digital content through Family Library.

To learn about content eligibility and how to add members to your Amazon Family, visit: Amazon Family

Family Library helps you share digital content with eligible members added to your Amazon Family.

You can link two adult Amazon accounts to share eBooks, audiobooks, apps, and games.

Adults are able to share Prime Video streaming access, Amazon Music, and Twitch Prime with Teens in their Amazon Family. They can share eBooks, apps, and games with children.

They can control and personalize each child’s experience. Select what content your children can access, and set educational goals and time limits.

To share your content:

  1. Go to the Content page and choose Books.
  2. Select the title that you wish to share and select Add to Library. A new window opens up to give you the Family Library options. Note: Teen logins in your Amazon Family can’t share content through Family Library.

Important: Child profiles that are inactive for more than 18 months are automatically removed. You’ll no longer be able to access a child profile after it’s removed. To remove a child profile, or to manage inactive child profile removal, go to your child profile’s information page at: Manage your Amazon Family

Miscellaneous

Add Highlights in Kindle for Web

Add colored highlights to text. Highlights synchronize to all your Kindle apps and devices.

  1. Select the text that you want to highlight.
  2. In the pop-up, select a color. The text is highlighted with your chosen color.

Note: Highlights you’ve made in Kindle for Web are displayed in a supported color on other devices and applications. For example, Kindle e-reader displays highlights in gray.

Add Notes in Kindle for Web

Notes synchronize to all your Kindle apps and devices.

  1. Select the text that you want to annotate.
  2. In the pop-up, select the Note icon.
  3. In the Note window, add your text.
  4. Click Save. The selected text is highlighted and note icon appears at the end.

Amazon Business Kindle Content Purchasing Policy

We may enable you to purchase digitized content from the Kindle Store for others through your Amazon Business account. All redemptions of digitized content purchased from the Kindle Store, such as books (“Kindle Content”), are subject to the Kindle Store Terms of Use. Each copy of Kindle Content you purchase may only be redeemed by a single Amazon.com user account and cannot be revoked or transferred by you after redemption. You may not resell the Kindle Content redemption links or any other entitlement to the Kindle Content. If we terminate your access to Amazon Business or you otherwise violate this Agreement or the Kindle Store Terms of Use, we may disable any unredeemed Kindle Content that you have purchased.

Ask Alexa to Read Back Your Most Recent Request

To have Alexa read back your most recent request from the last 60 seconds, say “Tell me what you heard?”

You can also say things like:

  • “What’s the last thing you heard?”
  • “What did I say?”
  • “What did you hear?”
  • “Repeat what you heard”
  • “Did you hear me?”

Changes to Amazon Newsstand

Effective March 9, 2023, Amazon stopped selling new subscriptions of Print and Kindle magazines and newspapers.

You can no longer subscribe to magazines or newspapers through Amazon. Amazon is no longer offering individual subscriptions. To explore possible subscription options, please visit the periodical’s Publisher Offer details page.

Kindle Newsstand

Note: Starting April 22, 2026, each magazine issue you borrow through Kindle Unlimited counts toward your 20-title borrow limit. We also no longer automatically deliver new issues.

After the end of Kindle Newsstand subscriptions, you can still:

  • Borrow magazines as part of a Kindle Unlimited membership.
  • Continue to access issues for your Kindle Newsstand subscription that were previously delivered to your Kindle App (iOS / Android) and e-reader library.
  • Download past issues to your Kindle library on the web: 1. In your web browser, go to Gestionar contenido y dispositivos and log into your Amazon account. 2. Select the Content tab, and then select either Magazine or Newspaper. 3. Locate the title that you want and select Deliver Past Issue.

Print Newsstand

After the end of Print Newsstand subscriptions, you can still:

  • Continue to receive Print Newsstand subscriptions, including gift subscriptions, that extend past March 2024. If your subscription term extends beyond March 2024, you’ll receive any remaining issues of the subscription you purchased on Amazon.
  • If your term has expired and you wish to resubscribe, you can explore Publisher Offer details for Digital & Print subscription options. Note: To cancel Print Newsstand subscriptions, or edit your delivery address, please contact the publisher directly. You can find publisher contact information on their website to address all customer service inquiries. For more information, please visit the Publisher Website details page to locate your publisher.

Changes to Kindle Vella

Kindle Vella closed on February 26, 2025.

Any stories and episodes you previously read or unlocked with tokens remain available in your Kindle app library.

To read your stories, download the free Kindle app for iOS, Android, or Fire. Stories are no longer available to read with Kindle for Web.

Content Guidelines for Books

These guidelines apply to book content, including title, cover art, and product description.

As a bookseller, we believe that providing access to the written word is important, including content that may be considered objectionable. We carefully consider the types of content we make available in our stores and review our approach regularly, listening to feedback and investigating concerns from our customers. We reserve the right to remove content from sale if we determine it creates a poor customer experience.

Authors, publishers, and selling partners are responsible for adhering to our content guidelines. We invest significant time and resources to enforce these guidelines, using a combination of machine learning, automation, and dedicated teams of human reviewers. We’ll remove content that does not adhere to these guidelines and promptly investigate any book when notified of potential noncompliance. If we remove a title, we let the author, publisher, or selling partner know and they can appeal our decision.

Illegal or Infringing Content
We take violations of laws and proprietary rights very seriously. It is the responsibility of authors, publishers, and selling partners to ensure their content doesn’t violate laws or copyright, trademark, privacy, publicity, or other rights. We will not accept content under copyright that is freely available on the web unless it’s provided by the owner of the copyright. In addition, we do not allow companion books based on copyrighted works (e.g., summaries, study guides, etc.) to be published outside the U.S. without written permission from the copyright holder.
Offensive Content
We don’t sell certain content including content that we determine is hate speech, promotes the abuse or sexual exploitation of children, contains pornography, glorifies rape or pedophilia, advocates terrorism, or other material we deem inappropriate or offensive.
Poor Customer Experience

Books for sale on Amazon should provide a positive customer experience. We do not allow descriptive content meant to mislead customers or that doesn’t accurately represent the content of the book. We also do not allow content that’s typically disappointing to customers, including the examples provided in the Guide to Kindle Content Quality.

Public Domain Content
We allow content that is in the public domain and no longer under copyright. However, to provide a better customer experience, we won’t allow undifferentiated versions of public domain titles if a free version is available in our store (as outlined in our policies for Publishing Public Domain Content). We may ask publishers to provide proof that the content is in the public domain.

Guidelines for other categories of products can be found here:

  • Offensive and Controversial Materials
  • Community Guidelines
  • Amazon Web Services
  • Prime Video Direct

Customize Kindle for Web

Tailor your reading experience to one that suits you.

You can modify the following reading features:

  • Font: Choose from Bookerly, Amazon Ember, and OpenDyslexic fonts.
  • Font Size: Choose from 14 sizes for all fonts.
  • Page Color: Choose from four options to change text and background colors.
  • Layout: Choose to display text in one or two columns.
  • Margin: Choose a narrow, medium, or wide margin.
  • Reading Progress: Select to show time left in chapter, page in book, and/or time left in book.

To change the Kindle for Web reading feature settings:

  1. Open a book and find the Aa icon at the top of the screen. Note: If the menu bar with the icon isn’t visible, navigate to the top of the app to reveal it.
  2. Select the Aa icon.
  3. Adjust your settings.
  4. To close the notebook, select the X.

Note: Changes synchronize to other Kindle for Web apps on desktop and mobile browsers.

Edit Notes in Kindle for Web

Changes synchronize to all your Kindle apps and devices.

  1. To open a note, click the Note icon.
  2. Change the text as required.
  3. Click Save.

Alternatively, you can edit a note by clicking the Notebook icon and clicking the Pencil icon beside the note.

Enable Kindle Reading Ruler

Turn on the Kindle Reading Ruler to gain focus and read more easily.

You can use the Reading Ruler on the Kindle apps for iOS, Android, and Fire tablets.

  1. Open the Kindle app.
  2. Select a book to read.
  3. While reading, tap the center of the screen to display the reading toolbar.
  4. Select the Aa icon then the More tab.
  5. Select Reading Ruler and then turn it On. Note: You will be prompted to enable continuous scrolling for Android and Fire tablets. You can also enable continuous scrolling in the Layout tab of the Aa menu.
  6. Select from the available options for color, opacity, style, and size.

Find Kindle Books in the Alexa App

To find your eligible Kindle books that Alexa can read, use the Alexa app.

Note: Kindle Assistive Reader isn’t available on Alexa+.

Important: Turn on the Kindle Assistive Reader to access your list of supported Kindle books. To turn on your Kindle Assistive Reader, follow the instructions on our Help page Turn On Kindle Assistive Reader on Echo Devices with a Screen.

To find your supported Kindle books in the Alexa app:

  1. Open the Alexa app Screenshot: Alexa app icon.
  2. Select More .
  3. Select Music & More .
  4. Under Kindle Library, select a book from the list.
  5. Choose an available device that you want Alexa to read from.

How Do I Link or Unlink My Goodreads and Amazon Accounts?

Linking your Goodreads and Amazon accounts allows you to track your reading progress for Kindle Reading Challenges.

To link or unlink your Goodreads and Amazon accounts:

  1. Go to Goodreads.com, and then Account Settings.
  2. Select Apps and then either Connect Amazon account or Disconnect Amazon account.
  3. Sign in with your Amazon account credentials.

Note: If you use the Kindle app for iOS, you can link accounts by going to the More menu. Join Goodreads and sign in with your Goodreads account credentials.

  • Learn About Kindle Reading Challenges

Identify Which Kindle Books Have Been Read

Easily separate books you have already read from those books still in progress or not yet started.

Kindle books automatically display as Read when you turn the last page.

On your smartphone:

  1. Select Library on your device.
  2. Use the Filter button to select either Read or Unread.

On your tablet:

  1. Select Read or Unread directly from the sidebar navigation.

Kindle Content Frequently Asked Questions

You can use this page to find answers to popular Kindle questions.

Q: What is Kindle Unlimited?

A: Kindle Unlimited is a service that allows you to read as much as you want from over 4 million titles, thousands of audiobooks, and magazine subscriptions. Explore new authors, books, and genres from mysteries and romance, to sci-fi and more. You can read on any device. It’s available for $11.99 USD/month + any applicable taxes until cancelled. Cancel anytime. If you haven’t tried Kindle Unlimited yet, go to Kindle Unlimited to try for free for 30 days.

Q: How can I access Kindle Unlimited books?

A: You can find Kindle Unlimited books everywhere you shop for Kindle books today. Look for the Kindle Unlimited icon throughout the Kindle Store and click on the “Read for Free” button on Kindle book pages to start reading.

You can find popular titles and personalized recommendations on the Kindle Unlimited page or you can browse our entire Kindle Unlimited catalog and refine by your favorite genre.

Q: Do I need a Kindle device to enjoy Kindle Unlimited?

A: No. You can read Kindle Unlimited books on your phone, tablet or computer with our free Kindle app. To download, visit Kindle Apps.

Q: How can I access Kindle Unlimited audiobooks?

A: You can enjoy Kindle Unlimited audiobooks on Kindle Fire HDX tablets or iOS and Android devices with our free Kindle (Kindle Apps) or Audible (How to Listen) apps. Look for the Kindle Unlimited “With Narration” icon on Kindle book pages or browse our catalog of titles with free audiobook companions.

Q: How can I access the current issue of a magazine?

A: You can find Kindle Unlimited magazine subscriptions within the Kindle Unlimited store. Click on the “Read for Free” button on Kindle magazine pages in the Kindle app, Amazon shopping app for Android, or your mobile/desktop browser to download your chosen magazine and start reading. At this time, you cannot borrow directly from the Amazon mobile shopping app for iOS or on your eReader, but you can read any downloaded magazines on all devices.

Q: Do I need to pay separately for magazine subscriptions in Kindle Unlimited?

A: No. A select range of magazine subscriptions is included with your Kindle Unlimited membership at no additional cost. You can access the current issue of each magazine you are subscribed to in Kindle Unlimited by visiting your library. You will have access to borrowed issues until you return them, you cancel your Kindle Unlimited membership, or the title is removed from Kindle Unlimited.

Q: Are Public Domain titles included in Kindle Unlimited? If so, why?

A: Around 500 public domain titles are included in Kindle Unlimited, all of which we have synced with their free audiobook companions as a benefit to Kindle Unlimited subscribers.

Q: How can I sign-up for Kindle Unlimited on my iOS or Android device?

A: To sign-up for Kindle Unlimited on your Android, iPhone or iPad, visit Kindle Unlimited in your browser. For Android users, you can also sign-up for Kindle Unlimited on your Android device by downloading the free Kindle app (Kindle Apps), tapping the “More” menu, then selecting “Kindle Unlimited Membership,” and following the sign-up process.

Q: Is Kindle Unlimited available to customers living outside the US?

A: Kindle Unlimited is currently available only to US customers on Amazon.com. Customers in the UK, Germany, France, Italy, Spain, India, Japan, Brazil, Mexico, Canada and Australia can sign up for Kindle Unlimited in their local Amazon marketplace.

For more information about Kindle Unlimited, visit our help page (Learn About Kindle Unlimited) and terms and conditions (Kindle Unlimited Terms of Use).
